# Unit and integration test cases (generated)

Generated by `compliance/tools/build_test_specs.py` from the test sources (read with `ast`). Identifier of a test case: `<file>::<function>`; a parametrized case runs once per parameter set. Inputs, expected outputs and the pass criterion of each case are its code: the fixtures and data it uses, and its assertions. A case passes when it runs to the end with every assertion true.

Levels: *unit* runs without Basilisk or Qt; *integration* runs a Basilisk simulation or the Qt GUI (offscreen).

**2381 test functions in 193 test designs** (1392 integration, 989 unit). 2381 have a docstring stating their purpose; 0 do not (their purpose is their name). 114 name the requirements they verify with a `requirement` marker; the others trace through the SRS validation matrix (SRS section 6).

## Test cases

### `tests/gui/test_app.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_main_sets_the_desktop_file_name_to_match_the_installed_desktop_entries` | main() sets the desktop file name the installed .desktop entries use, so shells find the icon. | - | integration |
| `test_desktop_entry_declares_a_matching_startup_wm_class` (parametrized) | Each .desktop entry sets StartupWMClass=spacemissionstudio. | - | integration |
| `test_desktop_file_name_matches_the_desktop_entries_own_basename` | The three places a ".desktop"-matching identifier is spelled out -- app.py's setDesktopFileName() call, and each installed .desktop file's own filename -- must agree, or the match this whole mechanism depends on silently | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_app_shell.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_source_of_names_templates_files_and_new_scenarios` | A template is "template NN", any other file its name, a scenario never saved nothing. | - | integration |
| `test_the_top_bar_names_the_scenario_and_its_state` | Template 22 as shipped reads "Unchanged · from template 22 · not run yet". | - | integration |
| `test_the_top_bar_says_saved_for_a_saved_file` | After File > Save As to a file of the user's, the bar says "Saved" and names that file. | - | integration |
| `test_the_chip_follows_a_finished_run` | Idle at start; "Running" while busy, with the elapsed time; "Run 1: done in ..." afterwards, and the top bar keeps the run's name, time, state and length (U-22). | - | integration |
| `test_the_chip_shows_a_live_run_percentage` | A run that reports progress shows its percentage on the chip. | - | integration |
| `test_the_chip_shows_failed_and_cancelled_runs` (parametrized) | A failed or cancelled run leaves its state on the chip and in the top bar's last-run text. | - | integration |
| `test_the_chip_rejects_an_unknown_state` | Only the states the theme styles are accepted. | - | integration |
| `test_the_status_bar_items` | The qualified Basilisk; all reference data OK or how many files need attention (with a warning style); Earth orientation with its high accuracy end date, or none and the fallback Earth-fixed frame. | - | integration |
| `test_the_reference_data_item_opens_the_data_dialog` | Clicking the reference data entry in the status bar opens the Reference data dialog, where the files are listed; the main tabs stay where they were. | - | integration |
| `test_the_status_bar_follows_the_data_tab` | A refresh of the reference data panel (after a download, import or rollback) refreshes the status bar's summary too. | - | integration |
| `test_a_closed_window_is_freed` | Nothing the window connects keeps it alive once it is closed and dropped. | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_budget_widget.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_budget_inputs_round_trip` | Every input survives the form, including "from last run" (None). | - | integration |
| `test_the_spacecraft_editor_keeps_the_budget` | Opening a spacecraft with budget inputs and pressing OK keeps them. | - | integration |
| `test_the_budget_tab_shows_the_table` | Template 18 at 400 km (no disposal burn needed): rows per phase and a total, with the collision-avoidance count noted as missing. | - | integration |
| `test_the_sweep_table_marks_the_worst_launch_and_shows_its_budget` | One row per launch date; the worst (2031 here) is bold and its budget fills the main table; picking another row shows that one. | - | integration |
| `test_the_sweep_button_runs_in_the_background_and_reports_progress` | "Launch delays" runs the sweep on a worker thread with progress, and reads "Cancel" while it runs. | - | integration |
| `test_the_trade_table_marks_the_lowest_fitting_altitude_and_drills_down` | One row per altitude; the lowest that fits the tank is bold and its launch dates (worst first) fill the sweep table; another row shows its own. | - | integration |
| `test_unreadable_altitudes_are_refused_before_any_work` | Altitudes that are not numbers are refused before any work starts, saying what is expected. | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_command_palette.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_every_word_must_match_and_name_prefixes_come_first` | Every typed word must match; matches at the start of a name come first, menu names count, and an empty search lists everything. | - | integration |
| `test_keywords_find_an_entry_by_another_name` | An entry's keywords match like its name but are not shown: "events", the old tab's name, finds the Timeline. | - | integration |
| `test_enter_runs_the_highlighted_command_and_skips_disabled_ones` | Enter runs the highlighted command, and a disabled one cannot be highlighted. | - | integration |
| `test_nothing_matching_says_so` | A search that matches nothing says so, and running then does nothing. | - | integration |
| `test_the_main_window_lists_its_commands_and_areas_on_ctrl_k` | Ctrl+K opens the palette, which lists the registry's commands, the areas and templates but not itself; "events", the old tab's name, finds the Timeline, and going there shows it. | - | integration |
| `test_the_palette_shows_a_series_of_the_result` | The palette offers each result series, and choosing one shows it on the Results page. | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_compare_tab.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_a_new_run_is_compared_with_the_one_before_it` | A new run is ticked with the one before it and compared with it. | - | integration |
| `test_the_summary_has_a_delta_per_run_against_the_oldest_ticked` | The summary has each run's figure and its difference from the oldest ticked run, units, and right-aligned numbers. | - | integration |
| `test_the_inputs_that_differ_from_the_base_old_to_new` | The inputs table lists each input that differs from the base run, per run; fewer than two runs ticked asks for two. | - | integration |
| `test_the_events_are_counted_with_their_total_time` | Events are counted with their total time per run and their difference from the base. | - | integration |
| `test_the_plots_overlay_one_series_of_every_ticked_run` | The plot overlays the chosen series of every ticked run, the base solid and the others dashed. | - | integration |
| `test_runs_of_an_earlier_session_are_listed_and_read_back_when_ticked` | The main window lists the saved runs at start-up and reads a run's series from disk only when it is compared. | - | integration |
| `test_the_shown_run_cannot_be_deleted_and_an_unreadable_one_is_named` | The run on show cannot be deleted; a run whose file cannot be read is named. | - | integration |

| `test_cached_fetch_is_suggested_only_once_the_source_is_local_file` | Real bug, found on a real user's machine: the cached path was pre-filled for EVERY scenario, so opening this dialog on a bundled -source scenario and clicking OK wrote the user's own absolute cache path into it (and into | - | integration |

| `test_harmonics_checkbox_off_by_default_forces_point_mass` | Spherical harmonics are off by default, giving a point mass (degree 0). | - | integration |
| `test_harmonics_checkbox_preserves_spinner_value_when_toggled_off` | Regression guard for the actual UX fix this dialog makes: unchecking "enable spherical harmonics" must not reset/lose the degree the user typed -- only fold it to 0 in the OUTPUT (to_gravity()), leaving the spinner itsel | - | integration |
| `test_checking_harmonics_with_zero_degree_bumps_to_a_sane_default` | Checking spherical harmonics at degree 0 sets a positive degree. | - | integration |
| `test_a_central_body_without_a_field_disables_and_clears_harmonics` | Choosing the Moon (no gravity field) disables and clears spherical harmonics, giving degree 0. | - | integration |
| `test_mars_offers_harmonics_up_to_the_ggm2b_degree` | Mars keeps spherical harmonics enabled, its degree box stops at GGM2B's 80 (a higher Earth degree is clamped to it), and Earth gives back GGM03S's 180. | - | integration |
| `test_loading_existing_harmonics_degree_checks_the_box` | A scenario with degree 8 opens with harmonics checked at 8 and gives 8 back. | - | integration |
| `test_accept_with_valid_state_closes_dialog` | OK with valid settings accepts the dialog. | - | integration |
| `test_atmosphere_model_defaults_to_nrlmsise00` | The atmosphere model defaults to NRLMSISE-00. | - | integration |
| `test_selecting_exponential_atmosphere_model_round_trips` | Choosing the exponential atmosphere gives it back. | - | integration |
| `test_exponential_atmosphere_model_disables_space_weather_controls` | Real user question this answers: 'why can't I select the drag model' -- once they can, switching to the model that has no F10.7/Ap dependence at all should visibly grey out the controls that only apply to NRLMSISE-00, no | - | integration |
| `test_solar_activity_offers_nominal_conservative_and_low` | One choice: nominal (MSFC 50th), conservative (95th, ESA AD10 operations) or low (5th); nominal by default. | - | integration |
| `test_srp_location_pointer_label_is_present` | Real user report: looked for solar radiation pressure in this dialog specifically and didn't find it -- this label is the fix (see this module's own docstring); regression guard that it doesn't silently disappear in some | - | integration |
| `test_record_interval_round_trips_and_zero_reads_every_step` | A 600 s record interval comes back, and 0 shows as Every step and gives 0. | - | integration |
| `test_forecast_percentile_and_msfc_file_round_trip` | AD10's 95th-percentile operations setting and a study's own MSFC file survive opening and OK-ing the dialog. | - | integration |
| `test_albedo_is_offered_for_bodies_with_an_albedo_map` | Earth and Mars have albedo maps: the box is enabled there, round-trips into the gravity settings, and is cleared and disabled for the Moon. | - | integration |

| `test_switching_series_redraws` | Showing the velocity series plots its three components with a velocity axis in m/s. | - | integration |

| `test_save_plot_as_png_writes_a_real_png_file` | End-to-end: real plotly.js rendering a real PNG in the offscreen QWebEngineView, not a mocked JS call -- the same "confirmed directly in this sandbox, not assumed" discipline this module's own docstring already holds its | - | integration |

| `test_save_plot_as_png_is_a_no_op_while_a_poll_is_already_in_flight` | Save PNG while an earlier save is still being written opens no dialog. | - | integration |

| `test_save_plot_as_svg_writes_a_real_svg_file` | End-to-end mirror of test_save_plot_as_png_writes_a_real_png_file, for the SVG path added alongside PNG (roadmap item M2) -- real plotly.js rendering in the offscreen QWebEngineView, not a mocked JS call. | - | integration |

| `test_redraw_does_not_reenable_save_svg_button_while_a_poll_is_in_flight` | SVG counterpart of test_redraw_does_not_reenable_save_button_while_a_png_poll_is_in_flight -- save_svg_button must stay disabled across a live-update _redraw() too, for the same reason. | - | integration |
| `test_series_colors_palette_is_8_distinct_valid_hex_colors` | Regression guard for roadmap item M3 (docs/ux_roadmap.md): `_SERIES_COLORS` is a specific, externally-validated-by-hand 8-hue palette (see that constant's own comment for the exact `scripts/validate_palette.js` run and n | - | integration |
| `test_the_grid_opens_on_a_single_series_plot` | Without a saved layout a result opens on one series plot, not the access timeline. | - | integration |
| `test_the_access_timeline_is_a_catalog_entry_under_link` | The access timeline is one more plot in the catalog (it used to be a separate view); shown, it has no series of its own. | - | integration |
| `test_access_timeline_plots_one_trace_per_pair_with_a_segment_per_window` | The access timeline draws one trace per station-spacecraft pair, with one segment per access window and a placeholder for a pair that never sees the spacecraft. | - | integration |
| `test_access_timeline_with_no_access_series_shows_explanatory_empty_state` | Without access series the catalog does not offer the timeline, and the timeline itself explains why it is empty. | - | integration |
| `test_access_timeline_ignores_x_axis_combo_always_uses_elapsed_time` | The access timeline stays in elapsed hours when the epoch x-axis is chosen. | - | integration |
| `test_switching_back_to_single_series_restores_previous_plot` | Going from the access timeline back to a series plots that series as before. | - | integration |
| `test_access_timeline_renders_in_the_real_webview` | End-to-end check that the access-timeline figure's generated HTML actually loads in the real (offscreen) QWebEngineView without error -- the unit-level trace-count assertions above don't catch a figure that builds fine i | - | integration |
| `test_save_plot_default_name_is_access_timeline_in_that_view` | Saving from the access timeline suggests access_timeline.png. | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_populates_the_catalog_on_first_update` | The first live update fills the plot catalog, enables Export and plots three components. | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_does_not_reset_users_series_selection` | Regression guard: a live run's series names are fixed from its first chunk (see set_live_result's docstring) -- later chunks with MORE data but the SAME series names must not rebuild the catalog, which would silently sna | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_grows_the_plotted_data` | A later live update with more samples plots more points. | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_throttles_rapid_webview_redraws` | Real user report: during a live-updating run, switching the Series dropdown appeared to do nothing. | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_eventually_pushes_after_the_throttle_window` | A live update after the 300 ms redraw interval redraws the page once. | - | integration |
| `test_user_series_change_is_never_throttled_during_a_live_run` | A user-initiated series change must redraw immediately even while still inside a live run's throttle window -- switching series is exactly the interaction the throttle must never block. | - | integration |
| `test_clicking_a_plot_name_in_the_catalog_redraws_the_plot` | Real user report (from the old series box): picking a series left the OLD plot on screen. | - | integration |
| `test_typing_an_exact_series_name_and_pressing_enter_redraws_the_plot` | Same real report, other path: typing a plot's title in the catalog's search and pressing Enter shows it. | - | integration |
| `test_position_series_plots_in_raw_meters` | Regression guard for a real, explicit user request ("state vector elements shall be displayed in meters for position and m/s for velocity") that REVERSED this module's own earlier km-conversion decision for exactly these | - | integration |
| `test_velocity_series_plots_in_raw_m_s` | Velocity is plotted unconverted, in m/s. | - | integration |
| `test_delta_v_series_always_plots_in_m_s_not_km_s` | Regression guard for the explicit "delta-V shall always be displayed in m/s" request -- delta-V shares the literal "m/s" unit string with velocity, which is exactly why the old blanket unit-string-keyed conversion got th | - | integration |
| `test_altitude_and_semi_major_axis_series_plot_in_km` | "altitudes, semi-major axes shall be displayed in km" -- both are recorded in raw meters by engine.service (matching every other Basilisk-facing SI field), so the km conversion is display-only here. | - | integration |
| `test_mean_orbital_element_series_is_plotted_and_labeled_distinctly_from_osculating` | A mean inclination is plotted in degrees and titled as a mean (first-order J2) element. | - | integration |

| `test_long_wrapping_series_is_thinned_evenly_not_min_max` | Min-max thinning would pick ~0 and ~360 deg from every stretch. | - | integration |
| `test_dimensionless_series_is_not_unit_converted` | An uncategorized dimensionless series is plotted unconverted under its own name. | - | integration |
| `test_single_column_series_has_no_legend` | dataviz principle: a single series names itself in the chart title -- no legend box is needed (unlike a >= 2 column series, e.g. | - | integration |
| `test_multi_column_series_has_a_legend` | A series with several columns shows a legend. | - | integration |
| `test_axes_never_use_scientific_notation` | Regression test for the real complaint that started the Plotly migration: matplotlib's default axis formatter fell back to scientific/offset notation on several of this app's own plots. | - | integration |
| `test_default_x_axis_is_elapsed_time_in_hours` | The x-axis defaults to elapsed time in hours. | - | integration |
| `test_epoch_x_axis_converts_time_s_to_datetimes` | With the epoch x-axis, sample times become UTC dates from the run's epoch, within a millisecond (TDB seconds converted to UTC). | - | integration |
| `test_epoch_x_axis_falls_back_to_elapsed_time_without_a_known_epoch` | Without an epoch, the epoch x-axis falls back to elapsed time. | - | integration |
| `test_epoch_x_axis_falls_back_to_elapsed_time_on_unparseable_epoch` | With an epoch that cannot be read, the epoch x-axis falls back to elapsed time. | - | integration |
| `test_switching_x_axis_back_to_elapsed_time_restores_it` | Switching from the epoch x-axis back to elapsed time restores elapsed time. | - | integration |
| `test_live_result_carries_epoch_through_to_the_plot` | A live result's epoch is used by the epoch x-axis. | - | integration |
| `test_set_live_result_rebuilds_the_catalog_if_series_names_change` | A live update with other series names rebuilds the catalog and shows the new series. | - | integration |
| `test_a_large_series_still_shows_when_selected` | Real user report: during a long run, switching to chief-1.position_N or .velocity_N "just doesn't change" -- each was a ~5.8 MB page and QWebEngineView.setHtml() silently shows nothing above 2 MB, so the previous plot st | - | integration |
| `test_display_thinning_keeps_peaks_and_caps_points` | Each plotted line is thinned to at most _MAX_PLOT_POINTS_PER_LINE points, keeping every stretch's minimum and maximum, so a short burn or spike still shows. | - | integration |
| `test_series_list_shows_plot_titles_with_the_code_name_as_tooltip` | Catalog entries read like their plot titles (without the spacecraft when the run has one), and the code name (the CSV file name) stays available as the entry's tooltip. | - | integration |
| `test_typing_a_full_code_name_still_selects_that_series` | Typing a series' full code name in the catalog search and pressing Enter shows it. | - | integration |
| `test_the_access_timeline_is_offered_only_with_access_series` | With no ground stations there is no timeline to offer; a later result without stations drops it from the grid too. | - | integration |
| `test_results_tab_fits_a_narrow_pane` | A one-line provenance label and a wide toolbar used to force the tab to ~780 px, wider than a typical right-hand pane. | - | integration |
| `test_plot_page_fills_the_view_without_a_scroll_bar` | Plotly's page kept the browser's default body margin under a 100%-height plot, so every plot had a scroll bar beside it. | - | integration |
| `test_access_timeline_marks_each_pass_and_starts_at_zero` | On a long run a pass is narrower than a pixel, so its segment alone vanished; fixed-size end ticks keep it visible. | - | integration |
| `test_suggestions_offer_the_featured_series_and_open_on_the_first` | The suggestion chips offer the featured series the result has, in order and without repeating the only spacecraft's name, and the first one is shown and checked. | - | integration |
| `test_clicking_a_suggestion_shows_it_even_from_the_access_timeline` | Clicking a suggestion chip from the access timeline shows that series and checks the chip. | - | integration |
| `test_suggestions_keep_names_that_tell_spacecraft_apart_and_hide_when_empty` | With two spacecraft, the chips keep the spacecraft names; the row hides without featured series or without a result. | - | integration |
| `test_newly_named_series_get_plot_titles` | Featured in templates 19/20 but shown as raw code names before. | - | integration |
| `test_a_poll_tick_after_the_poll_finished_is_ignored` | SRelD K-08: a timer tick queued before the poll finished used to read the cleared state ("'NoneType' object is not subscriptable"). | - | integration |
| `test_only_one_poll_query_is_in_flight_and_late_answers_are_ignored` | SRelD K-08: on a slow machine several queries were in flight at once; each answer finished the save again (a second write and "saved" dialog). | - | integration |
| `test_a_pending_answer_lets_the_next_tick_ask_again` | While the plot is still rendering, each answer frees the next tick to ask again, until the result arrives. | - | integration |
| `test_an_answer_that_never_comes_does_not_stall_the_save` | One query at a time must not mean waiting forever: if an answer is lost, the poll asks again after a second of ticks. | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_run_worker.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_run_without_basilisk_emits_failed` | Without Basilisk, the run worker fails saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | integration |
| `test_live_run_without_basilisk_emits_failed` | The live=True path fails the same way as live=False -- the ImportError happens before either run()/run_live() is ever reached (see RunWorker.run()'s try block). | - | integration |
| `test_monte_carlo_worker_without_basilisk_emits_failed` | Without Basilisk, the Monte Carlo worker fails saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | integration |
| `test_run_always_uses_run_live_and_passes_should_cancel` | Confirms the non-mission_sequence path is always chunked via run_live() now (for cancellability), regardless of live=False -- see this module's own docstring on why. | - | integration |
| `test_live_false_never_emits_progress` | A run that is not live signals no progress. | - | integration |
| `test_live_true_emits_progress` | A live run signals each progress update. | - | integration |
| `test_request_cancel_makes_should_cancel_true` | A cancel request is seen by the run's cancel check. | - | integration |
| `test_simulation_cancelled_emits_cancelled_signal_with_partial_result` | A cancelled run signals cancellation with its partial result and no summary. | - | integration |
| `test_mission_sequence_dispatch_passes_should_cancel_to_mission_engine` | A mission-sequence run hands the worker's cancel check to the mission engine. | - | integration |
| `test_mission_engine_cancelled_emits_cancelled_signal_with_partial_result_and_summary` | A cancelled mission-sequence run signals cancellation with its partial result and summary. | - | integration |
| `test_a_basilisk_exit_during_a_run_is_reported_not_silent` | Audit H2: Basilisk's device factories call exit() (SystemExit) on settings they refuse. | - | integration |
| `test_monte_carlo_worker_runs_a_single_thread_batch` | A batch with one thread succeeds from the GUI's worker thread. | - | integration |
| `test_monte_carlo_worker_reports_the_batch_error` | An error the batch raises (here: a folder holding other files) reaches the worker's failed signal with its message. | - | integration |
| `test_monte_carlo_child_logs_reach_this_process` | An unexpected error in the child process comes back as a BatchProcessError, and the child's log record of it reaches this process's loggers (the Log view and the log file listen there). | - | integration |

| `test_dialog_resizes_to_its_own_sizehint_on_construction` | See test_constellation_dialog.py's identical test for why. | - | integration |
| `test_trail_and_ground_tracks_default_off_and_round_trip` | The flown-path trail and ground tracks build up into a solid band over a long run (real user feedback), so both start unticked. | - | integration |

### `tests/gui/test_vizard_launcher.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_find_returns_none_with_no_remembered_path_and_nothing_on_disk` | With nothing remembered and nothing in the search folders, no Vizard is found. | - | integration |
| `test_remember_then_find_returns_the_remembered_path` | A remembered Vizard executable that exists is the one found. | - | integration |
| `test_remembered_path_that_no_longer_exists_falls_back_to_search` | A remembered executable that no longer exists is skipped, and the search finds another. | - | integration |
| `test_search_finds_executable_directly_in_a_candidate_root` | The search finds the executable directly in a search folder. | - | integration |
| `test_search_finds_executable_one_level_down_in_a_candidate_root` | Covers "unzipped into its own subfolder" (e.g. | - | integration |
| `test_search_skips_a_nonexistent_root_without_raising` | A missing search folder ahead of a real one is skipped without an error. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_starts_the_given_executable_directly_on_linux` | On Linux, the executable is started directly, without arguments, and its process returned. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_appends_direct_comm_flag_when_given` | See DEFAULT_LIVE_STREAM_ADDRESS's own comment: -directComm is what makes Vizard connect to a live-stream run automatically instead of sitting on its own manual launcher screen. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_omits_direct_comm_flag_when_not_given` | Without a direct-communication address, Vizard is started without that flag. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_treats_an_app_bundle_path_like_any_other` | macOS is not supported (2026-10-21): a path ending in .app is no longer resolved to a binary inside it, but started as given, on every platform, even one that reports itself as macOS. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_no_longer_inspects_an_empty_app_bundle` | An empty .app folder used to be refused as "not a valid macOS app bundle"; with macOS unsupported it is not inspected, and starting it is left to the operating system, as for any other path. | - | integration |
| `test_download_url_for_platform_covers_windows_and_linux` | Windows and Linux each get their own Vizard download, and there is no macOS one (macOS is not supported, 2026-10-21). | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_downloads_extracts_and_finds_the_executable` | fetch_vizard downloads the zip, extracts it and returns the executable inside (network mocked). | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_sends_a_browser_like_user_agent` | Regression guard: urllib's own default User-Agent ("Python-urllib/<version>") got "HTTPError: 403 Forbidden" from a real host on a real user's machine -- asserts the actual outgoing urllib.request.Request carries a real  | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_sets_the_executable_bit_on_non_windows` | fetch_vizard makes the extracted Linux executable executable by its owner. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_reports_network_failure` | A network error during fetch_vizard is reported as a download failure. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_reports_a_corrupt_zip` | A download that is not a zip file is reported as such. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_reports_a_zip_with_no_recognizable_executable` | A zip without a Vizard executable is reported: the executable could not be found. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_honors_cooperative_cancellation` | fetch_vizard stops with a cancelled error when its cancel check says so. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_rejects_a_response_over_the_size_cap` | A download larger than the size limit is refused. | - | integration |
| `test_a_cancelled_or_oversized_download_leaves_no_partial_file` | Audit H4: the download streams to a .part file, which is removed when the download stops early, so nothing half-written is left behind. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_reports_a_folder_it_cannot_create` | Audit H4: a destination under a file is a VizardFetchError the dialog can show, not an OSError that would end the worker thread silently. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_reports_progress_status` | fetch_vizard reports downloading and extracting through its status callback. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_records_the_download_with_its_sha256` | No checksum is published for Vizard, so the download is recorded (source, size, SHA-256) and the hash is shown to the user (security analysis S-05). | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_finds_the_executable_inside_a_same_named_wrapper_folder` | Matches AVS's own real .zip layout -- Vizard_<platform>.zip wraps its contents in a same-named top-level folder. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_clears_stale_files_from_an_earlier_extraction` | A second fetch removes the first extraction and returns the new executable. | - | integration |
| `test_fetch_vizard_rejects_a_zip_slip_entry` | A zip entry that would extract outside the folder (../) is refused as unsafe. | - | integration |
| `test_vizard_fetch_worker_emits_finished_ok_on_success` | The fetch worker signals success with the executable's path. | - | integration |
| `test_vizard_fetch_worker_emits_failed_on_error` | The fetch worker signals failure with the error's message. | - | integration |
| `test_vizard_fetch_worker_reports_an_unexpected_error` | Audit H4: any exception in the worker reaches the user through ``failed``; before, only VizardFetchError did and the dialog waited forever. | - | integration |
| `test_vizard_fetch_worker_request_cancel_is_seen_by_should_cancel` | A cancel requested before the worker starts is seen by the fetch's cancel check. | - | integration |
| `test_launch_opens_a_playback_file_with_load_file` | Vizard's documented ``-loadFile`` argument opens a run's playback file (UX/UI guidelines, decision 3: open only). | - | integration |
| `test_the_playback_file_follows_basilisks_naming` | vizSupport.enableUnityVisualization: a ``.bin`` path is used as is; any other name goes to ``_VizFiles/<name>_UnityViz.bin`` beside it. | - | integration |

| `test_each_kvn_syntax_and_segment_rule_is_enforced` (parametrized) | An Annex G example broken in one way gives an issue citing the rule (TIME_SYSTEM outside the 3.2.3.2 set is a warning: it needs an ICD). | CCSDS-502.0-B-3 3.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.4.3, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.4.4, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.5.1, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.5.4, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.5.8, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.8.5, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 3.2.3.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.2.1, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.3.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.2.1, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.3.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.4.1.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.8.8, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.9.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 7.9.2.3, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.4.4, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.5.3, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.5.7 | unit |
| `test_the_written_gm_is_the_gm_the_propagation_uses` | OPM elements and the CLI's element-to-state conversion use the Earth GM of the simulation (Basilisk 2.12: 398600.436 km^3/s^2), not GGM03S's. | CCSDS-502.0-B-3 3.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 5.2.2.2, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 3.1.2 | integration |

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |

| `test_every_offered_body_runs_as_a_third_body` | A run around Earth with every other offered body as a third body finishes. | - | integration |

### `tests/test_cli.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_validate_accepts_a_valid_scenario` | validate on a valid scenario exits 0 and prints OK. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_rejects_an_invalid_scenario` | validate on a scenario with an empty name exits 1 and prints INVALID to stderr. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_rejects_malformed_json` | validate on a file that is not JSON exits 1 and prints INVALID to stderr. | - | unit |
| `test_spaceweather_resolve_reports_resolution` | spaceweather-resolve exits 0 and says what the scenario's space weather resolved to, from the bundled CelesTrak SW-All data. | - | unit |
| `test_spaceweather_resolve_reports_a_clean_error_instead_of_a_traceback` | Regression test for an audit finding: cmd_spaceweather_resolve() had no exception handling around sw.resolve(), unlike every other command in this file -- a SpaceWeatherError (e.g. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_writes_new_scenario` | generate-constellation writes a scenario with 6 uniquely named spacecraft on classical elements and says how many it generated. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_append_keeps_existing_spacecraft` | generate-constellation --append keeps the original spacecraft beside the generated ones. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_requires_template_spacecraft_flag_when_ambiguous` | With two spacecraft in the file and no --template-spacecraft, generate-constellation exits 1 asking for the flag. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_uses_named_template_spacecraft` | generate-constellation copies the spacecraft named by --template-spacecraft. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_rejects_unknown_template_spacecraft_name` | A --template-spacecraft the file does not have exits 1, naming it. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_constellation_rejects_invalid_walker_parameters` | 10 satellites in 3 planes (not divisible) exits 1 with INVALID. | - | unit |
| `test_run_without_basilisk_reports_clear_error` | Without Basilisk, run exits 2 saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | unit |
| `test_kernels_status_without_basilisk_reports_clear_error` | Without Basilisk, kernels-status exits 2 saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_notes_script_blocks` | validate says a scenario has script blocks and that run needs --allow-scripts for them (SRS-S-03). | - | unit |
| `test_run_refuses_script_blocks_without_allow_scripts` | Without --allow-scripts, run stops before building anything, with exit code 1 and the block's path (SRS-S-03). | - | unit |
| `test_run_with_allow_scripts_runs_them` | With --allow-scripts the script block runs. | - | integration |
| `test_run_rejects_both_vizard_flags_at_once` | run with both --vizard-save-file and --vizard-live-stream exits 1: at most one is allowed. | - | unit |
| `test_help_opens_with_a_one_line_purpose_for_users` | ``spacemissionstudio --help`` starts with what the tool does, examples and where the guide is, not the module's developer docstring. | - | unit |
| `test_version_prints_tool_basilisk_and_python_versions` | ``spacemissionstudio --version`` (and ``-V``) prints three lines: the tool's version, the Basilisk line and Python, exits 0, and logs nothing (no "Logging to" line on stderr). | - | unit |
| `test_version_says_when_basilisk_is_missing_or_not_qualified` | The Basilisk line names the qualified version when another one is installed, and says simulations cannot run when none is. | - | unit |
| `test_version_before_a_command_prints_the_versions_and_exits` | ``--version`` given with other arguments goes through the parser: it still prints the versions to stdout and exits with status 0. | - | unit |
| `test_run_parses_vizard_camera_and_orbit_line_flags` | run parses --vizard-camera-target and --vizard-no-orbit-lines. | - | unit |
| `test_run_vizard_camera_target_defaults_to_none` | Without the flags, run has no Vizard camera target and draws orbit lines. | - | unit |
| `test_station_keeping_summary_reports_delta_v_and_propellant_used` | The station-keeping summary gives the final cumulative delta-V, the propellant used and the propellant left. | - | unit |
| `test_station_keeping_summary_includes_phasing_delta_v_breakdown` | A phasing follower's summary adds its altitude-keeping and phasing delta-V and lists both; the chief, without station keeping, is not summarized. | - | unit |
| `test_station_keeping_summary_skips_spacecraft_without_the_config` | A spacecraft without station keeping prints no summary. | - | unit |
| `test_monte_carlo_rejects_scenario_without_enabled_flag` | monte-carlo on a scenario with Monte Carlo off exits 1 saying so. | - | unit |
| `test_monte_carlo_without_basilisk_reports_clear_error` | Without Basilisk, monte-carlo exits 2 saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | unit |
| `test_no_subcommand_is_an_error` | The command line without a subcommand exits with an error. | - | unit |
| `test_unknown_scenario_file_is_a_clear_error` | validate on a file that does not exist exits 1 with INVALID. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_writes_new_scenario` | generate-phasing-formation adds a follower with phasing keeping 50 km from the chief, and station keeping, after the chief. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_takes_a_control_law` | --control-law puts a Basilisk formation law on the generated follower. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_rejects_zero_along_track_km` | generate-phasing-formation with a 0 km separation exits 1 with INVALID. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_rejects_unknown_chief_name` | generate-phasing-formation with a chief the file does not have exits 1 with ERROR. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_without_basilisk_reports_clear_error` | Without Basilisk, generate-phasing-formation exits 2 saying Basilisk is not installed. | - | unit |
| `test_generate_phasing_formation_passes_every_request_field_through` | Regression test: --eclipse-sunlit-threshold was missing from the CLI even though PhasingFormationRequest (and the GUI's own phasing dialog) support it. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_defaults_agree_between_schema_generator_and_cli` | One default per setting: the schema's PhasingKeepingConfig, the phasing-formation generator's request, and the CLI must not drift apart (the correction window used to be 21 days in all of them, and was shortened to 3 day | - | unit |
| `test_run_parses_vizard_trail_and_ground_track_flags` | --vizard-trail and --vizard-ground-tracks are off by default and on when given. | - | unit |
| `test_lifetime_reports_reentry_and_the_disposal_rules` | Template 18 at 400 km re-enters in March 2031 (about 15 months); with a burn down to a 200 km perigee, within weeks. | - | integration |
| `test_budget_prints_the_ad10_table` | Template 18 without a run: the table, a total and the notes. | - | integration |
| `test_budget_launch_delays_prints_one_row_per_launch_date` | ``budget --launch-delays``: the planned launch and 1-5 years late, the worst marked (drag and disposal stubbed: the real ones take a minute or two). | - | integration |
| `test_lifetime_drag_coefficient_defaults_to_ad10s_end_of_life_value` | lifetime's drag coefficient defaults to 2.2 (AD10's end-of-life value), takes 'own', and refuses a negative value. | - | unit |
| `test_budget_altitudes_prints_one_row_per_altitude` | ``budget --altitudes 400,500``: the worst launch date per altitude and whether it fits the tank (drag and disposal stubbed). | - | integration |
| `test_budget_altitudes_parse` | budget's --altitudes is unset by default, empty when given bare, takes a comma-separated list in km and refuses an altitude below the allowed range (40 km). | - | unit |
| `test_run_reports_an_output_folder_it_cannot_write` | Audit H3: an --out-dir that sits under a file is an error the user sees (exit status 3), not a traceback. | - | integration |
| `test_ccsds_import_reports_a_message_it_cannot_read` (parametrized) | Audit M12: a missing message file, or a folder, gave a traceback. | - | unit |
| `test_run_refuses_an_oem_stride_below_one` (parametrized) | Audit L7: --oem-stride 0 or -3 was accepted silently. | - | unit |
| `test_number_options_refuse_nan_and_infinity` (parametrized) | float() accepts "nan" and "inf": every number option refuses them with a usage error, before they reach Basilisk (generate-phasing-formation --radial-km nan ended in a Basilisk AttributeError). | - | unit |
| `test_budget_refuses_altitudes_with_launch_delays` | Audit L8: --altitudes silently overrode --launch-delays. | - | unit |
| `test_ctrl_c_and_unreported_file_errors_end_with_a_message` | Audit L10 and L9: Ctrl-C printed a traceback (now status 130), and a write error a command does not report itself gave one too (status 3). | - | unit |
| `test_validate_prints_the_plausibility_warnings` | Audit L11: validate printed none of the GUI's plausibility checks; a perigee below the ground is now a WARNING line (the status stays 0). | - | unit |

### `tests/test_command.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_default_command_has_no_children` | A command made without children has an empty child list. | - | unit |
| `test_unknown_kind_is_rejected` | An unknown command kind gives one error naming the kind and the command's path. | - | unit |
| `test_label_appears_in_error_paths` | A command's label is quoted in its error messages. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_duration_valid` | A propagate for a positive duration validates. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_duration_missing_is_rejected` | A duration propagate without duration_days is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_duration_zero_is_rejected` | A duration propagate of 0 days is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_epoch_valid` | A propagate to an ISO 8601 stop epoch validates. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_epoch_malformed_is_rejected` | A propagate to a stop epoch that is not a date is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_event_valid` | A propagate to a spacecraft's next periapsis validates. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_event_missing_spacecraft_is_rejected` | An event propagate without a spacecraft is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_pass_event_needs_a_ground_station` | A propagate to the start of a pass is refused without a ground station and validates with one. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_event_bad_kind_is_rejected` | An event kind the tool does not offer (sunrise) is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_propagate_bad_stop_condition_is_rejected` | A stop condition the tool does not offer is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_valid` | A maneuver with a spacecraft and a three-element delta-V validates. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_defaults_to_inertial_frame` | A maneuver without a frame validates: the frame defaults to inertial. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_missing_spacecraft_is_rejected` | A maneuver without a spacecraft is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_bad_delta_v_shape_is_rejected` | A two-element maneuver delta-V is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_bad_frame_is_rejected` | A maneuver frame the tool does not offer (lvlh) is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_maneuver_reports_every_problem_at_once` | The key requirement: a collecting validate(), not raise-fast. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_valid` | A Lambert transfer with a spacecraft, a target position and a time of flight validates. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_defaults_num_revolutions_and_constraints` | A Lambert transfer validates without revolutions, target distance or minimum radius: all are optional. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_missing_spacecraft_is_rejected` | A Lambert transfer without a spacecraft is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_bad_target_position_shape_is_rejected` | A two-element Lambert target position is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_missing_time_of_flight_is_rejected` | A Lambert transfer without a time of flight is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_non_positive_time_of_flight_is_rejected` | A Lambert transfer with a time of flight of 0 s is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_negative_num_revolutions_is_rejected` | A Lambert transfer with a negative number of revolutions is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_negative_min_orbit_radius_is_rejected` | A Lambert transfer with a negative minimum orbit radius is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_reports_every_problem_at_once` | An empty Lambert transfer reports the missing spacecraft, target position and time of flight together. | - | unit |
| `test_assignment_valid` | An assignment of a value to a dotted spacecraft field validates. | - | unit |
| `test_assignment_missing_value_is_rejected` | An assignment without a value is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_assignment_target_without_dot_is_rejected` | An assignment target without a dotted field is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_report_empty_series_is_valid` | A report without a series list validates. | - | unit |
| `test_report_bad_series_type_is_rejected` | A report whose series is a string, not a list, is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_conditional_requires_condition` | An if without a condition is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_non_conditional_kind_rejects_children` | A maneuver with children is refused: only conditionals and loops take children. | - | unit |
| `test_if_validates_children_recursively` | An if's invalid child is reported under the child's own path. | - | unit |
| `test_nested_if_validates_grandchildren` | A grandchild inside an if and a while is validated and reported under its full path. | - | unit |
| `test_script_block_valid` | A script block with code validates. | - | unit |
| `test_script_block_missing_code_is_rejected` | A script block without code is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_to_dict_via_asdict_and_from_dict_round_trip` | A command with a label, parameters and a child comes back equal through asdict and from_dict. | - | unit |
| `test_from_dict_defaults_missing_optional_fields` | from_dict with only a kind gives no label, no parameters and no children. | - | unit |
| `test_a_report_before_any_propagate_is_flagged` | The engine fails on such a report at run time ("no recorded samples yet") -- template 16 shipped that way; it is now caught up front. | - | unit |
| `test_a_report_after_a_propagate_is_fine` | Reports after a propagate, and an empty sequence, give no report-before-propagate error. | - | unit |
| `test_only_certain_cases_are_flagged` | A report inside if/while may never run, and a propagate nested in an earlier command may have run -- flagging either would block Run on a sequence that can work, so neither is an error. | - | unit |
| `test_scenario_validation_reports_it` | A report before any propagate in template 08 fails validation and is listed by validate_all. | - | unit |
| `test_conditions_evaluate_like_python` (parametrized) | The allowed subset gives Python's own value for each expression. | - | unit |
| `test_conditions_cannot_reach_beyond_their_values` (parametrized) | Attribute access, calls, lambdas and comprehensions are refused, so a condition from a scenario file cannot reach any Python object (finding S-02). | - | unit |
| `test_condition_errors_name_the_problem` | Unknown names list the available ones; a syntax error and a huge exponent are refused with a message. | - | unit |
| `test_a_condition_cannot_exhaust_memory_or_the_stack` (parametrized) | A crafted condition is refused with a message (security analysis S-12): too long or nested too deep (it raised MemoryError or RecursionError, which crashed loading the scenario; both are refused at validation), or repeat | - | unit |
| `test_chained_operations_cannot_build_a_huge_value` (parametrized) | Audit M2: the per-operation limits let a chain of operations build a 1 GB text or a billion-digit integer. | - | unit |
| `test_validation_reports_a_condition_outside_the_allowed_set` | The editor shows a disallowed condition before the run, with the command's path. | - | unit |
| `test_script_blocks_are_found_at_any_depth` | script_blocks() lists every script_block with its item path, so the CLI and the GUI can ask for consent naming each one (SRS-S-03). | - | unit |

### `tests/test_command_enabled.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|

| `test_templates_save_unchanged` | Every template saves back to exactly the text it was loaded from: nothing gains an ``enabled`` key. | - | unit |
| `test_a_non_boolean_enabled_is_an_error` | ``"enabled": "no"`` in a file names the command and the field. | - | unit |
| `test_a_disabled_command_is_still_validated` | Disabling a broken command does not hide its problem. | - | unit |
| `test_runs_sequence_needs_one_enabled_top_level_command` | A sequence whose commands are all disabled runs like an empty one. | - | unit |
| `test_disabled_scripts_need_no_consent` | Only scripts that run are listed for consent; paths keep their index in the file. | - | unit |
| `test_a_disabled_propagate_does_not_count_before_a_report` | A report after a disabled propagate still has nothing recorded. | - | unit |
| `test_disabled_propagates_do_not_count_toward_the_time_limit` | The one-run time limit adds up only the propagates that run. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_comms_pointing.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|

### `tests/test_constellation.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_generates_total_satellites_count` | A 12-satellite Walker request gives 12 spacecraft with unique names. | - | unit |
| `test_semi_major_axis_matches_altitude_plus_earth_radius` | Every satellite's semi-major axis is Earth's equatorial radius plus the 500 km altitude. | - | unit |
| `test_raan_spacing_is_360_over_planes_for_delta_pattern` | A delta pattern of 3 planes spaces them 360/3 = 120 deg apart in RAAN. | - | unit |
| `test_raan_spacing_is_180_over_planes_for_star_pattern` | A star pattern of 3 planes spaces them 180/3 = 60 deg apart in RAAN. | - | unit |
| `test_mean_anomaly_spacing_within_one_plane` | Four satellites in a plane are 90 deg apart in mean anomaly. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_factor_offsets_adjacent_planes` | With T=8, P=2, F=1 the second plane is offset by F*360/T = 45 deg in mean anomaly. | - | unit |
| `test_raan_offset_rotates_whole_constellation` | A 30 deg RAAN offset rotates every plane by 30 deg. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_uses_mean_anomaly_not_true_anomaly` | Generated orbits give the mean anomaly, not the true anomaly. | - | unit |
| `test_template_fields_other_than_name_and_orbit_are_preserved` | Every satellite keeps the template's other fields, with its own copy of the power model. | - | unit |
| `test_generated_names_are_deterministic_and_prefixed` | Names are prefix-plane-slot, numbered from 01. | - | unit |
| `test_generated_scenario_validates` | A scenario of the generated satellites validates and saves. | - | unit |
| `test_request_validation_rejects_bad_input` (parametrized) | Each bad request field is refused, naming it: zero satellites or planes, satellites not divisible by planes, a phasing factor of P or more, a zero altitude, an inclination over 180 deg, eccentricity 1, an unknown pattern | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_rejects_empty_distances` | A separation schedule without distances is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_single_entry_holds_for_whole_mission` | A one-entry schedule gives that separation, as an angle (distance / a), at any time. | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_steps_through_distances_on_schedule` | A three-entry schedule changes separation at each 90-day interval, not before. | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_holds_at_last_entry_when_not_looping` | Without looping, the schedule holds its last separation after its end. | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_loops_when_requested` | With looping, the schedule starts again from its first separation after its last. | - | unit |
| `test_separation_schedule_zero_interval_holds_first_entry` | A zero interval holds the first separation for the whole mission. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_data_handling.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_the_cosine_exponent_gives_the_peak_gain_as_the_patterns_directivity` | cos^n over the forward hemisphere: integrating the pattern numerically gives back the peak gain the exponent was derived from. | - | unit |
| `test_a_patch_loses_gain_off_boresight_and_floors_behind_its_ground_plane` | A cosine patch of 6.02 dBi (n = 1) has its peak on boresight, loses 3 dB at 60 deg, sits at its front-to-back floor from 90 deg, never rises off boresight and has a 120 deg half-power beam. | - | unit |
| `test_a_datasheet_table_interpolates_in_db_and_holds_its_last_gain` | A gain table interpolates in dB, holds its last gain beyond its end and gives its half-power beamwidth. | - | unit |
| `test_the_per_step_margin_equals_the_full_link_budget` | engine.data_handling evaluates margin_at_one_metre - 20 log10 r - pointing loss every step; it must agree with link_margin_db. | - | unit |
| `test_a_fixed_pattern_keeps_the_earlier_link_budget` | No pattern: the same numbers as before patterns existed. | - | unit |
| `test_a_data_handling_scenario_round_trips_through_json` | Data handling and the RF link survive JSON. | - | unit |
| `test_bad_data_handling_and_antenna_settings_are_refused_plainly` (parametrized) | Each bad data-handling or antenna setting is refused with a plain message. | - | unit |
| `test_an_attitude_dependent_pattern_is_refused_in_orbit_only_mode` | A patch pattern is refused in orbit-only mode; a fixed one is allowed. | - | unit |
| `test_the_explain_tab_gives_the_data_budget_before_any_run` | The Explain tab gives the daily data and the time to fill memory, the rate per pass, and warns when there is no downlink. | - | unit |
| `test_predicted_series_include_the_data_chain_and_the_link_gate` | The predicted series include the data chain and the link gate. | - | unit |
| `test_each_link_window_that_sent_data_is_one_downlink_event` | Each link window that sent data is one downlink event with the data sent. | - | unit |
| `test_the_run_summary_reads_the_totals_back` | The run summary gives the data generated, downlinked, lost and left on board. | - | unit |
| `test_the_memory_fills_refuses_data_and_every_bit_is_accounted_for` | Generated = downlinked + lost + on board; the memory never exceeds its capacity; data only leaves while the link closes. | - | integration |
| `test_what_the_ledger_counts_as_downlinked_is_what_the_transmitter_sent` | An independent check: a memory that never runs empty, so every bit the transmitter asks for leaves it -- the ledger's count must equal the transmitter's own output summed over the steps. | - | integration |
| `test_the_gate_measures_the_angle_between_boresight_and_station` | off_boresight_deg against a hand-made geometry: the station straight 'below' along -x of a body turned 90 deg about z. | - | integration |
| `test_the_memory_and_the_totals_carry_across_long_run_segments` | One day in one run, then as four segments: the memory's contents carry over and the generated, downlinked and lost totals match. | - | integration |
| `test_instruments_and_the_transmitter_draw_from_the_battery` | With the power budget on, the instruments' draw is constant and the transmitter's only while it sends: the battery ends lower than without them, by about what they drew. | - | integration |

| `test_css_saturation_max_clips_the_output` | A saturation of 0.1 clips the reading to 0.1. | - | integration |
| `test_css_fault_stuck_max_freezes_at_saturation_regardless_of_bias` | A stuck-at-maximum fault reads the saturation value whatever the bias. | - | integration |
| `test_css_unrecognized_fault_mode_raises_fsw_error` | An unknown coarse-sun-sensor fault mode is refused. | - | integration |
| `test_magnetometer_bias_adds_per_axis_to_the_true_field` | A magnetometer bias adds to each axis separately. | - | integration |
| `test_magnetometer_saturation_clips_every_axis_symmetrically` | A magnetometer saturation clips every axis to plus or minus its value. | - | integration |
| `test_magnetometer_fault_stuck_value_freezes_only_the_named_axis` | A stuck-value fault on axis 1 reads the stuck value there and leaves the other axes as they were. | - | integration |
| `test_magnetometer_unrecognized_fault_mode_raises_fsw_error` | An unknown magnetometer fault mode is refused. | - | integration |
| `test_magnetometer_out_of_range_fault_axis_raises_fsw_error_not_indexerror` | Real audit finding: fault_axis indexes a plain 3-element Python list (stuckValue/etc.) before being passed to Basilisk's own setFaultState(axis, state). | - | integration |
| `test_imu_gyro_bias_adds_to_the_true_rate` | The gyro reads the true rate, and a gyro bias adds to each axis. | - | integration |
| `test_imu_accel_bias_reads_directly_since_true_accel_is_zero` | This bare sim has no gravity/forces, so the true specific force is 0 -- a clean baseline where the accelerometer's reading is exactly its own bias, with nothing else to subtract out. | - | integration |
| `test_imu_gyro_saturation_clips_the_output` | A gyro saturation of 0.01 rad/s clips a 1 rad/s rate to 0.01. | - | integration |
| `test_imu_gyro_lsb_quantizes_the_output` | A coarse encoder step size (0.01 rad/s) forces the reported rate onto a multiple of that step -- the real true rate (0.014) is NOT itself a multiple of 0.01, so a quantized reading proves the LSB setting actually took ef | - | integration |
| `test_star_tracker_with_a_walk_bound_runs_and_produces_finite_output` | Unlike the other three kinds, bias_walk_bound_arcsec's effect is a STOCHASTIC long-run bound on an accumulated random walk, not something a single deterministic tick can demonstrate -- this test confirms the real Basilis | - | integration |

| `test_a_heliocentric_run_does_not_make_the_sun_shadow_itself` | Audit L27: around the Sun, the eclipse model listed the Sun as an occulting body of its own light. | E-ST-10-04C 6.2.1a, E-ST-10-04C 4.2.1b | integration |

| `test_nonzero_cross_track_offset_changes_orbital_plane` | A 5 km cross-track offset gives the follower a different inclination from the chief's. | - | integration |

| `test_along_track_separation_is_far_off_when_placed_via_a_single_hill2rv_call` | Negative control for the test above: confirms the OLD (buggy) approach -- a single hill2rv(chief_r, chief_v, [R, T, N]) call, then rv2elem straight back to classical elements -- really does misread a 500 km along-track r | - | integration |
| `test_along_track_separation_degrades_gracefully_with_nonzero_radial_and_cross_track` | R/N are layered on AFTER the along-track placement (see module docstring) -- the resulting along-track reading won't be exact anymore, but must stay in the right ballpark (nowhere near the total failure -- ~0 km -- the s | - | integration |
| `test_phasing_keeping_targets_the_along_track_km` | The along-track offset is the separation phasing_keeping holds. | - | integration |
| `test_station_keeping_is_required_and_set` | The follower gets station keeping with the request's default 5 kg of propellant. | - | integration |
| `test_default_target_altitude_is_derived_from_chief` | The station-keeping altitude defaults to the chief's semi-major axis minus Earth's radius (549.863 km for a = 6928 km). | - | integration |
| `test_explicit_target_altitude_overrides_derived_default` | A station-keeping altitude given in the request is used. | - | integration |
| `test_template_fields_other_than_orbit_are_cloned` | The follower copies the template's power model but takes its own name. | - | integration |
| `test_generated_follower_passes_full_scenario_validation` | The generated follower validates. | - | integration |
| `test_rejects_zero_along_track_km` | A 0 km along-track offset is refused. | - | integration |
| `test_rejects_follower_name_matching_chief_name` | A follower named like the chief is refused. | - | integration |
| `test_rejects_non_classical_elements_chief_orbit` | A chief on a Cartesian orbit is refused: it needs classical elements. | - | integration |
| `test_the_generated_follower_uses_the_requested_control_law` | A Basilisk formation law chosen in the generator lands on the follower's phasing_keeping, with the law's default gains. | - | integration |

### `tests/test_formation_control.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_drift_orbit_stays_the_default_and_older_files_still_load` | A scenario saved before the control_law field existed runs the drift-orbit controller, as before. | - | unit |
| `test_the_basilisk_laws_validate_and_round_trip` (parametrized) | Both Basilisk laws are valid on template 05 and survive a save and load. | - | unit |
| `test_an_unknown_law_or_a_non_positive_gain_is_rejected` (parametrized) | Each new field is checked, and the error names it. | - | unit |
| `test_mean_oe_needs_earth_j2_in_the_dynamics` | meanOEFeedback maps to mean elements with Earth's J2; with point-mass gravity that mapping would add a J2 motion the run does not have. | - | unit |
| `test_hill_pd_is_flagged_where_its_thruster_cannot_hold_the_separation` | Template 05's 0.05 N thruster on 405 kg gives 1.2e-4 m/s^2; holding 50 km with the Hill-frame law takes 6.5e-4 m/s^2 (3 n^2 a (1 - cos theta)). | - | unit |
| `test_hill_pd_is_flagged_when_it_starts_too_far_off_for_its_thruster` | A 1 km formation placed 50 m off: K * error = 2e-6 * 50 = 1e-4 m/s^2, under the thruster's 1.2e-4, so no warning; 5 km off (1e-2) is flagged. | - | unit |
| `test_firings_of_a_basilisk_law_become_burn_events` | State 4 (engine.formation_control.FIRING) is a "formation control firing". | - | unit |
| `test_only_the_basilisk_laws_add_the_force_series` | The commanded/applied force series exists only for the Basilisk laws. | - | unit |
| `test_mean_oe_pulls_a_5_km_error_in_and_holds_50_km` | meanOEFeedback, gain 2500 m^2/s^3 per kg: the follower placed at 55 km reaches 50 km within a day (2.6 m/s measured in the 5-day study in HISTORY.md) and then holds it to tens of metres. | - | integration |
| `test_hill_pd_holds_a_close_formation_within_its_thrust_limit` | hillFrameRelativeControl with Basilisk's documented gains holds a 1 km formation placed 50 m off to metres, without saturating the 0.05 N thruster. | - | integration |
| `test_a_request_above_the_thrust_is_cut_to_the_thrust` | A 5 km error makes the Hill-frame law ask for ~40 N; the bridge applies 0.05 N in the requested direction. | - | integration |
| `test_requests_under_half_a_minimum_impulse_bit_are_not_fired` | Holding on target, meanOEFeedback asks for ~1e-4 N: 0.06 s of a 0.05 N thruster per 30 s tick, under half of a 300 s minimum on-time, so the thruster stays off (ThrusterOnTimeModel). | - | integration |
| `test_no_thrust_in_eclipse_and_none_without_propellant` | The bridge's own gates, driven message by message: a 1 N request is cut to the thruster's 0.05 N in sunlight, and dropped in eclipse or with an empty tank. | - | integration |
| `test_the_hill_reference_lies_on_the_chiefs_circular_orbit` | The reference point is ``s`` of arc ahead on the chief's circle, not ``y = s`` on the straight along-track axis. | - | integration |
| `test_a_scenario_with_a_basilisk_law_builds_and_runs_through_the_service` | The service wires the chosen law for a real scenario (template 05 for ten minutes) and records its series. | - | integration |

### `tests/test_formation_keeping_regression.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_follower_does_not_reboost_alone_and_closes_toward_its_target` | The exact failing configuration: before the fix the follower's own station-keeping fired at t = 0.07 d and the follower ended day 1 about 500 km BEHIND the chief. | - | integration |
| `test_follower_mirrors_a_chief_reboost_so_the_formation_holds` | A narrow deadband on BOTH spacecraft makes the chief reboost at t = 0.07 d. | - | integration |
| `test_formation_follower_burn_logic` | A formation follower never reboosts on its own; it mirrors the chief's burn and ends with it, and burns alone only when more than the deadband below the chief, until level again. | - | integration |
| `test_zero_crossing_ignores_the_plus_minus_180_wrap` (parametrized) | A separation error crossing zero counts as reached; a wrap from -180 to +180 deg does not. | - | integration |
| `test_a_correction_burn_delivers_exactly_its_planned_delta_v` (parametrized) | A tenfold stronger thruster must not change how much delta-V a correction uses: each burn is capped to the delta-V it still needs within a task interval, never a whole tick at full thrust. | - | integration |
| `test_on_time_model_ideal_thruster_fires_only_the_delta_v_still_needed` | An ideal thruster gives the average thrust for exactly the delta-V still needed, with nothing left over. | - | integration |
| `test_on_time_model_rounds_a_small_burn_up_to_one_minimum_firing` | A burn of more than half a 300 s minimum firing is rounded up to the full firing, reports the extra delta-V and runs ten 30 s ticks. | - | integration |
| `test_on_time_model_skips_a_burn_below_half_an_impulse_bit` | A burn below half a minimum firing is skipped and reported as too small. | - | integration |
| `test_on_time_model_eclipse_cuts_a_firing_short` | Eclipse ends a firing at once. | - | integration |
| `test_on_time_model_open_ended_firing_outlasts_its_request_until_the_minimum_on_time` | An open-ended firing goes on for its 90 s minimum after the request stops. | - | integration |
| `test_eccentricity_gate_waits_for_the_balancing_side_of_the_orbit` | Eccentricity-neutral burns stop at u = 0 once the eccentricity change reaches its allowance and fire again on the opposite side, which cancels it. | - | integration |
| `test_shared_thruster_firing_belongs_to_the_controller_that_started_it` | Station-keeping and phasing share one thruster model. | - | integration |
| `test_a_trim_finer_than_one_impulse_bit_is_flown_as_an_exact_pair` (parametrized) | Rounding a sub-bit trim to whole firings left up to half a bit of semi-major-axis error (~130 m here), which drifts the formation by ~17 km/day, so a coarse thruster ran a full correction every day. | - | integration |
| `test_a_trim_within_tolerance_of_whole_firings_is_not_split` (parametrized) | A restore that needs just under one firing is flown as ONE firing (and a tiny one is skipped): a pair would cost three firings' worth of delta-V for an error already within the 25 m tolerance. | - | integration |
| `test_eccentricity_neutral_split_never_leaves_a_piece_shorter_than_the_minimum_on_time` | A 450 s burn with a 300 s minimum on-time is flown as ONE 450 s firing: splitting it into 300 s + 150 s would round the remainder up to a whole extra firing (or skip it). | - | integration |

| `test_should_cancel_checked_between_while_loop_iterations` | should_cancel is checked before each while-loop iteration's child command starts (_run_commands, re-entered once per iteration by _run_while) -- here with each iteration's propagate sized to finish in exactly one _advanc | - | integration |

### `tests/test_model3d.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_every_shape_mesh_is_closed_and_wound_outward` (parametrized) | Each shape's drawn mesh is watertight with outward normals: its signed volume is positive. | - | unit |
| `test_box_solid_and_shell_match_the_formulas` | A solid box: I = m (b^2 + c^2) / 12 per axis. | - | unit |
| `test_octagonal_prism_matches_the_regular_polygon_formula` | A solid regular n-gon prism of circumradius R: Izz = m R^2 (1 + 2 cos^2(pi/n)) / 6, Ixx = Izz / 2 + m h^2 / 12; its first flat faces +x. | - | unit |
| `test_curved_closed_forms_agree_with_finely_meshed_shapes` (parametrized) | The cylinder and cone closed forms against the exact integrals of a 256-sided mesh of the same shape: they agree to 0.1 %. | - | unit |
| `test_sphere_solid_and_shell_moments` | Solid sphere 2/5 m r^2, thin shell 2/3 m r^2. | - | unit |
| `test_combine_applies_the_parallel_axis_theorem` | Two 1 kg points at x = +-1 m: center at 0, Iyy = Izz = 2 kg m^2, Ixx = 0. | - | unit |
| `test_rotation_turns_the_inertia_and_products_appear` | A box turned 90 deg in yaw swaps Ixx and Iyy; turned 45 deg it gets the product Ixy = (Ixx_local - Iyy_local) / 2: negative for this box, whose long x side now runs along x = y (tensor convention, Ixy = -integral(x y dm) | - | unit |
| `test_rotation_matrix_is_yaw_then_pitch_then_roll` | [roll, pitch, yaw] = [0, 0, 90] turns +x onto +y; [90, 0, 0] turns +y onto +z; combined, roll acts about the already-yawed x axis (body +y), so the part's z turns onto body +x. | - | unit |
| `test_open_mesh_cannot_be_a_solid_but_can_be_a_shell` | A box missing one face is not closed: a solid is refused with the hint to use a shell, which works. | - | unit |
| `test_welding_closes_a_mesh_with_repeated_corners` | Corners repeated once per face (as many exporters write them) leave the mesh open until they are welded. | - | unit |
| `test_mirroring_scale_keeps_normals_outward` | Scaling by -1 along x mirrors the mesh; the winding is reversed so the volume stays positive. | - | unit |
| `test_box_faces_and_their_projected_areas` | A box's six faces: areas y*z, x*z, x*y with outward normals at the face centres; from +x it shows y*z. | - | unit |
| `test_curved_faces_carry_the_true_area` | A cylinder's side faces share its true side area, so from across the axis it shows close to diameter * height; a sphere shows close to pi r^2 from anywhere. | - | unit |
| `test_mesh_faces_merge_coplanar_triangles` | A meshed box gives 6 planes (its 12 triangles merged in pairs); a 32-sided cylinder gives 34; areas add up to the surface area. | - | unit |
| `test_obj_round_trip_keeps_groups_vertices_and_colours` | Write two groups, read them back: same names, vertices, triangles and colours. | - | unit |
| `test_obj_reader_handles_polygons_negative_indices_units_and_y_up` | A quad written with negative indices and v/t/n corners, in mm and Y-up: two triangles, metres, and the file's +y becomes +z. | - | unit |
| `test_obj_reader_names_the_bad_line` (parametrized) | Malformed files raise ObjError with the reason (and the line). | - | unit |
| `test_obj_reader_limits_and_material_paths` | Files over the size or triangle limit are refused; a material file in another folder is not opened. | - | unit |
| `test_merge_keeps_every_triangle` | Merging meshes offsets the second mesh's indices. | - | unit |

| `test_rename_spacecraft_raises_on_empty_new_name` | Renaming a spacecraft to an empty name is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_rename_spacecraft_to_same_name_is_a_harmless_no_op` | Renaming a spacecraft to its own name changes nothing and counts no references. | - | unit |
| `test_rename_ground_station_updates_self_and_references` | Renaming a ground station renames it and the spacecraft's pointing target. | - | unit |

|---|---|---|---|

| `test_result_set_add_and_export` | A result exports one CSV file per series. | - | unit |
| `test_result_set_provenance_defaults_to_none` | A new result has no provenance. | - | unit |
| `test_result_set_export_csv_without_provenance_writes_no_sidecar` | Without provenance, export writes no provenance.json. | - | unit |
| `test_result_set_export_csv_with_provenance_writes_sidecar_json` | With provenance, export writes provenance.json holding every provenance field. | - | unit |

| `test_conservation_drift_warnings_too_few_samples_has_no_warnings` | A single sample gives no warning. | - | unit |
| `test_conservation_drift_warnings_detects_injected_energy_and_momentum_drift` | A speed that grows 10 % over one orbit gives both an energy and an angular-momentum warning naming the spacecraft. | - | unit |
| `test_conservation_drift_warnings_respects_custom_tolerance` | A 0.1 % drift passes the default 1 % tolerance but trips a 1e-6 tolerance on both quantities. | - | unit |
| `test_command_summary_export_csv_long_format` | The command summary's CSV has one row per report, series and component, with the label (empty when none) and the values in order. | - | unit |
| `test_command_summary_export_csv_creates_parent_dir` | The command summary's CSV export creates missing parent folders. | - | unit |
| `test_command_summary_csv_is_utf8_whatever_the_locale` | Audit L12: the Command Summary CSV was written in the locale's encoding, so on Windows (cp1252) a label it cannot encode raised and other text came out garbled. | - | unit |
| `test_command_summary_export_csv_with_no_reports_writes_header_only` | A command summary without reports writes only the header. | - | unit |
| `test_conservation_drift_warnings_stay_short` | Each warning is one scannable line in the Results tab (they used to be ~330-character paragraphs); the explanation lives in a tooltip. | - | unit |

| `test_output_path_accepts_a_plain_file_name` | A plain file name, spaces and non-ASCII letters included, is joined onto the output folder unchanged. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_roe.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_deputy_elements_invert_the_roe` | From ROE to the deputy's elements and back gives the same ROE, to a nanometre. | - | unit |
| `test_identical_orbits_have_zero_roe` | A deputy on the chief's own orbit has all six ROE zero. | - | unit |
| `test_an_equatorial_chief_is_refused` | The inclination vector needs a node: an equatorial chief raises. | - | unit |
| `test_the_j2_stm_matches_secular_propagation_of_both_orbits` (parametrized) | The J2 STM predicts the ROE that the two spacecraft's mean elements, each moved with its own secular J2 rates, give: within 0.1 m over 10 days for a formation of a few hundred metres. | - | unit |
| `test_the_keplerian_stm_misses_the_j2_drift` | Without J2 the same prediction is tens of metres off after 3 days (the e/i vectors turn and the nodes drift), so the J2 test above really tests the J2 terms. | - | unit |
| `test_without_j2_only_the_mean_longitude_drifts` | The Keplerian STM moves only dlambda, at -3/2 n da. | - | unit |
| `test_rtn_motion_follows_the_roe` | The first-order relative position: an e vector gives a radial oscillation of its size, an i vector a cross-track one, and dlambda an along-track offset. | - | unit |
| `test_the_in_plane_pair_makes_the_requested_change` | Two along-track burns half an orbit apart change da and the e vector by exactly the requested amounts (Gauss's equations), for the least delta-V. | - | unit |
| `test_burns_at_other_locations_still_make_the_change` | Three along-track burns away from the optimal angles (say, the optimal ones are in eclipse) still give the exact change, for more delta-V than the optimal pair. | - | unit |
| `test_the_cross_track_burn_turns_the_inclination_vector` (parametrized) | One cross-track burn, at the change's angle or half an orbit later with the opposite sign, makes the inclination-vector change, for n a /d(di)/. | - | unit |
| `test_no_change_needs_no_burns` | A zero change plans no burns. | - | unit |
| `test_the_drift_brings_the_mean_longitude_to_its_target` | Holding the planned da for the window moves dlambda to its target, J2 included (checked with the STM over the window). | - | unit |
| `test_a_capped_drift_takes_longer` | With the drift's da capped below what the window needs, the plan holds the cap for longer instead. | - | unit |
| `test_a_safe_formation_has_parallel_e_and_i_vectors` | The design helper puts the e and i vectors at the same phase, with the requested sizes, and the follower at the requested distance. | - | unit |
| `test_the_safety_margin_is_the_closest_radial_cross_track_approach` (parametrized) | The passive-safety formula equals the minimum over an orbit of the radial/cross-track distance, from the first-order motion. | - | unit |
| `test_inside_the_tolerances_nothing_is_planned` | A formation inside every tolerance gets no burns and no drift. | - | unit |
| `test_an_along_track_error_starts_a_drift` | A mean-longitude error out of tolerance starts a drift: along-track burns set the drift's da, which the STM says closes the error in the window. | - | unit |
| `test_a_small_drift_is_shorter_not_smaller` | A drift whose da would be below what the burns set (a quarter of the da tolerance) holds that da for less time instead. | - | unit |
| `test_a_drift_in_progress_holds_its_da` | While drifting, the drift's da is kept: no burns while it holds. | - | unit |
| `test_a_stopped_drift_returns_da_to_the_target` | Stopping a drift brings da back to the target's, and the drift ends. | - | unit |
| `test_eclipsed_locations_are_avoided` | With the optimal locations in eclipse, the along-track burns move to sunlit ones and the cross-track burn half an orbit on, and still make the change. | - | unit |
| `test_a_long_burn_is_split_over_orbits` | A burn larger than one part is split into equal parts at the same location on consecutive orbits; the parts come in time order. | - | unit |
| `test_burns_too_close_together_wait_an_orbit` | A burn closer than the minimum separation to one already scheduled waits an orbit; one too close ahead also waits an orbit. | - | unit |
| `test_the_sequence_dl_shift_is_the_delayed_da` | A da change made t seconds from now leaves dl 1.5 n d(da) t further than one made now (the Keplerian STM over the same span). | - | unit |
| `test_the_in_plane_sequence_also_lands_dl` | Four along-track burns over two orbits change da and the e vector and bring dl to its target by the last burn: stepped through with the Keplerian ROE motion (dl moves at -3/2 n da between burns), unlike the pair, which l | - | unit |
| `test_without_j2_drag_moves_dl_by_three_quarters_n_da_dot_t_squared` | With J2 off, a steady decay moves da by da_dot t and dl by -3/4 n da_dot t^2 on top of the Keplerian drift. | - | unit |
| `test_the_drag_column_matches_a_stepped_j2_propagation` | Over a day under J2, the drag column's change of every ROE matches stepping the J2 STM in one-minute steps with da growing between them (the chief's elements moved on with the secular rates): the decay's effect, with and | - | unit |
| `test_the_estimator_finds_the_decay_across_burns` | Burns that move da by metres (told to the estimator) and a chief reboost it is not told about (a 13 m jump) do not throw the fit: over a day the decay comes out within 1 %. | - | unit |
| `test_the_estimator_waits_for_four_orbits_of_data` | No estimate until the stretches together span four orbits. | - | unit |
| `test_the_estimator_forgets_what_left_its_window` | When the decay changes, a day later only the new one is fitted. | - | unit |
| `test_the_estimator_keeps_one_sample_a_minute` | Ticks closer together than the sample interval are skipped, unless they start a new stretch. | - | unit |
| `test_only_a_decay_that_matters_is_compensated` | The decay is compensated when it would move dl by more than half its tolerance over the window: 3.5 m a day would move it 800 m in a day, the scatter twin spacecraft show (0.03 m a day) 7 m, under the 20 m tolerance's ha | - | unit |
| `test_a_drag_cycle_swings_dl_evenly_inside_its_tolerances` (parametrized) | Started from the cycle's da and dl, the Keplerian motion with the decay keeps dl and da inside six tenths of their tolerances for the whole cycle (2 t*), dl back where it started and da on the other side, so the next cor | - | unit |
| `test_no_decay_means_no_drag_cycle` | With no decay the cycle changes nothing and never ends. | - | unit |
| `test_the_in_plane_sequence_lands_dl_under_drag` | With the decay passed in, the burns bring da, the e vector and dl where they were asked to by the last burn, stepped through with the decay acting between them. | - | unit |
