| `test_the_continuous_law_reconfigures_for_more_delta_v` | Basilisk's meanOEFeedback reaches the same helix: over the last half day every ROE error is under 5 m. | - | integration |
| `test_the_results_have_the_expected_series_and_events` | Every series engine.series_names promises is recorded, and the burns and the drift appear as events. | - | integration |

| `test_twin_spacecraft_compensate_no_drag` | Template 05's sister satellites feel the same drag: the estimate stays under a tenth of a metre a day and nothing is compensated, so the hold flies as it did before the compensation existed. | - | integration |

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|

| `test_sso_detection_does_not_fire_for_an_unrelated_inclination` | 51.6 deg at the same altitude is not shown as Sun-synchronous. | - | unit |
| `test_template_05_shaped_chief_follower_pair_produces_spacecraft_table` | A chief and a phasing follower, both with station keeping, give a two-row table, station- and phasing-keeping badges without a warning note, and one formation diagram with the follower's target and tolerances. | - | unit |
| `test_formation_diagram_uses_the_first_entry_of_a_multi_step_schedule` | The formation diagram shows the first separation of a schedule. | - | unit |

| `test_no_formation_diagram_without_phasing_keeping` | A scenario without phasing keeping has no formation diagram. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_without_station_keeping_surfaces_a_note` | A follower with phasing keeping but no station keeping gets a note naming it. | - | unit |
| `test_explain_does_not_raise_on_any_bundled_template` (parametrized) | Every template is explained under its name. | - | unit |
| `test_every_string_in_the_explanation_stays_short` (parametrized) | Permanent regression guard: this module's whole design point is terse, scannable fragments, not prose -- a real user explicitly rejected two earlier, more verbose designs before this one. | - | unit |
| `test_explain_never_raises_even_on_a_scenario_missing_its_own_schema_defaults` | A scenario with an empty name and epoch and no spacecraft is still explained. | - | unit |

| `test_identical_spacecraft_share_one_table_row` | A 6-satellite constellation used to repeat the same row six times. | - | unit |
| `test_badges_use_plain_names_and_reserve_status_colours` | Real user feedback on clutter: Drag/SRP badges were in the warning colour and power/RF in the success colour, though neither is a state; attitude modes showed code names; stations were a count plus a list. | - | unit |
| `test_sso_inclination_without_oblateness_is_not_called_sun_synchronous` | The precession that makes an orbit Sun-synchronous comes from J2; templates 01/09/10/12 use a Sun-synchronous inclination with point-mass gravity, where the plane never turns -- the badge would be a false claim. | - | unit |

| `test_the_altitude_is_above_the_scenarios_own_central_body` | Audit L21: the orbit tile subtracted Earth's radius around any body; a 1837.4 km orbit around the Moon is 100 km up, not -4541 km. | - | unit |
| `test_a_failing_section_leaves_the_rest_of_the_tab` | Audit L21: one section raising blanked the whole Explain tab, with nothing logged; now only that section is left out, and it is logged. | - | unit |
| `test_an_roe_formation_is_explained` (parametrized) | The formation section names the ROE law, says each target in words (with its closest radial/cross-track approach), and carries one e/i-vector diagram with every target. | - | unit |
| `test_differential_drag_is_explained` (parametrized) | A follower with three times the chief's drag area is told to feel differential drag, with what its law does about it; the twins of template 05 are not. | - | unit |

| `test_optical_navigation_warns_about_disk_size_and_pointing` | A narrow camera sees the disk larger than the image at periapsis, a coarse one too small at apoapsis; with attitude, a camera not pointed at the central body, or along another axis than locationPointing's, is flagged. | - | unit |
| `test_the_small_body_section_gives_spin_orbit_and_pixel_size` | Around Eros: the gravity model, its 5.27 h spin, the orbit's period in turns of Eros, and the landmark camera's 13.9 m per pixel at the lowest point (30 deg over 1024 px from 35 km, above Eros's 8.42 km mean radius); the | - | unit |
| `test_a_point_mass_eros_or_an_unpointed_camera_is_flagged` | Point-mass gravity around Eros, and a camera with attitude but no pointing at Eros, each get a warning badge. | - | unit |
| `test_missing_eros_files_are_checked_before_running` | Without the Eros ephemeris and shape the run would stop at once: 'Check before running' says so. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_scenario_robustness.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_a_wrong_typed_value_is_refused_naming_the_field` (parametrized) | A value of the wrong type in a template file is refused, naming the field and what it got. | - | unit |
| `test_a_condition_that_would_exhaust_memory_is_refused_on_load` | A shared scenario whose if-condition nests 100 000 levels deep raised MemoryError while loading; now it is refused with the reason. | - | unit |
| `test_mutated_templates_load_or_are_refused_never_crash` | 400 random single changes to the bundled templates (a value replaced by a wrong type, a key removed or added): each loads, or is refused with ScenarioValidationError. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_scenario_schema.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_minimal_scenario_validates` | A scenario with only a name, an epoch and one spacecraft on classical elements validates. | - | unit |
| `test_round_trip_save_load` | Saving and loading keeps the name, epoch, description, spacecraft orbit and schema version. | - | unit |
| `test_round_trip_preserves_every_field_category` | Gravity settings, ground stations and sensor parameters survive a save and load. | - | unit |
| `test_saved_file_is_plain_readable_json` | A saved scenario is plain JSON that json.loads reads without a custom decoder, with the current schema version. | - | unit |
| `test_scenario_level_validation_errors` (parametrized) | An empty name and an epoch that is not ISO 8601 are refused with a message naming the problem. | - | unit |
| `test_duration_days_at_the_100_day_cap_validates` | A 100-day run validates. | - | unit |
| `test_duration_days_beyond_ten_years_rejected` | A run longer than 3660 days (ten years) is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_a_run_past_one_basilisk_run_validates` | Past ~104 days Basilisk's nanoToSec() returns NaN, so such a run is split into segments (engine/long_run.py); the schema allows it. | - | unit |
| `test_a_mission_sequence_past_one_basilisk_run_is_rejected` | A mission sequence cannot be split into segments. | - | unit |
| `test_zero_spacecraft_rejected` | A scenario without a spacecraft is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_duplicate_spacecraft_names_rejected` | Two spacecraft with the same name are refused. | - | unit |
| `test_unsupported_central_body_rejected` | A central body the tool does not offer (Pluto) is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_spherical_harmonics_on_a_body_without_a_field_rejected` | Spherical harmonics around a body without a gravity field (the Moon) are refused by the schema, as engine.service would refuse them at run time (an audit finding: `validate` and Save passed such a scenario). | - | unit |
| `test_mars_takes_the_ggm2b_field_up_to_its_degree` | Mars takes spherical harmonics up to GGM2B's degree 80, not beyond. | - | unit |

| `test_invalid_eccentricity_rejected` | An eccentricity of 1.2 (hyperbolic) is refused: the schema takes 0 <= e < 1. | - | unit |

| `test_tle_orbit_requires_both_lines` | A TLE orbit with only its first line is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_classical_elements_orbit_defaults_to_true_anomaly` | Classical elements take the true anomaly unless told otherwise, and validate. | - | unit |

| `test_constant_thrust_rejects_negative_propellant` | Constant thrust with negative propellant is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_constant_thrust_allowed_alongside_station_keeping` | Independent propellant budgets -- see ConstantThrustConfig's docstring -- so both may be set on the same spacecraft at once. | - | unit |
| `test_simulation_mode_defaults_to_full_attitude` | The simulation mode defaults to full_attitude, and the default validates. | - | unit |
| `test_simulation_mode_rejects_unknown_value` | A simulation mode the tool does not offer is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_rejects_fsw_mode` | An FSW mode in an orbit-only run is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_rejects_sensors` | Sensors in an orbit-only run are refused. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_rejects_actuators` | Actuators in an orbit-only run are refused. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_rejects_power` | A power model in an orbit-only run is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_allows_station_keeping_and_constant_thrust` | Neither needs an attitude model -- see Scenario.simulation_mode's docstring -- so both remain usable in orbit_only mode. | - | unit |
| `test_orbit_only_mode_allows_plain_cannonball_spacecraft` | An orbit-only spacecraft with drag and SRP validates. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_defaults_to_none` | Phasing keeping is unset by default, and a spacecraft without it validates. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_round_trips_through_save_load` | Phasing keeping's chief and target separation survive a save and load, with the default tolerance kept. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_requires_station_keeping_on_same_spacecraft` | Phasing keeping on a follower without station keeping is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_rejects_chief_being_itself` | A follower that names itself as its chief is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_rejects_unknown_chief_spacecraft` | A chief the scenario does not have is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_rejects_empty_target_separation` | Phasing keeping without a target separation is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_rejects_non_positive_target_separation` | A target separation of 0 km in the list is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_rejects_bad_tuning_knobs` (parametrized) | Each phasing-keeping tuning value out of range (a negative reconfiguration interval, or a zero tolerance, window, drift time or semi-major-axis step) is refused, naming the field. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_keeping_full_scenario_validates` | A chief and a follower with station keeping and phasing keeping validate. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_defaults_to_empty_list` | The mission sequence is empty by default. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_empty_does_not_affect_existing_validation` | An empty mission_sequence (every scenario written before this field existed) must validate exactly as before -- this is the whole point of it being additive/opt-in. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_round_trips_through_save_load` | A mission sequence with nested commands, labels and parameters survives a save and load. | - | unit |
| `test_scenario_validate_raises_on_first_bad_command` | A maneuver command without a spacecraft makes the scenario fail validation. | - | unit |
| `test_scenario_validate_raises_on_dangling_command_reference` | A maneuver on a spacecraft the scenario does not have is refused, naming it. | - | unit |
| `test_scenario_validate_accepts_valid_mission_sequence` | A propagate followed by a maneuver on an existing spacecraft validates. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_preserves_command_order_through_round_trip` | mission_sequence is walked IN ORDER by the (not-yet-written) execution engine, so order is semantically load-bearing, not incidental -- explicitly locked in here, on top of the general round-trip test above. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_insert_and_reorder` | Reordering (insert-before/-after, drag-to-reorder in the eventual GUI) is just ordinary list manipulation on mission_sequence -- this locks in that Scenario doesn't do anything surprising (sorting, deduplication, etc.) t | - | unit |
| `test_direction_vectors_reject_zero_and_non_finite_values` (parametrized) | Regression test for an audit finding: a zero or NaN body-frame axis passed validation (only its LENGTH was checked) and only failed mid-run, once normalized into a NaN attitude target. | - | unit |
| `test_station_keeping_rejects_an_invalid_min_on_time` (parametrized) | A negative, NaN or implausibly long (1e6 s) thruster minimum on-time is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_station_keeping_thruster_realism_fields_default_to_an_ideal_thruster_when_absent_from_json` | A file without min_on_time_s and eccentricity_neutral_burns loads as an ideal thruster: no minimum on-time, single burns. | - | unit |
| `test_forecast_percentile_must_be_one_msfc_publishes` | MSFC publishes the 95th, 50th and 5th percentiles only. | - | unit |
| `test_reaction_wheel_settings_basilisk_would_exit_on_are_refused` (parametrized) | Reaction-wheel settings on which Basilisk's rwFactory would exit the process (an unknown type, non-positive or non-numeric u_max or Js, a non-boolean useMaxTorque, a zero betaStatic, a label over 5 characters, a motor be | - | unit |
| `test_thruster_settings_basilisk_would_exit_on_are_refused` (parametrized) | Thruster settings on which Basilisk's thrusterFactory would exit the process (an unknown type, a zero direction, non-positive thrust or Isp, a non-finite position, a negative minimum on-time) are refused. | - | unit |
| `test_a_torque_rod_needs_a_positive_dipole` | A torque rod with a negative maximum dipole is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_the_wheel_and_thruster_type_lists_match_basilisk` | The schema stays Basilisk-free, so it lists the factory types itself; they must be exactly the factories' own build methods. | - | unit |
| `test_names_that_cannot_be_file_names_are_refused` (parametrized) | Audit H3: a spacecraft name becomes an output file name, so one that climbs out of the output folder or is not a legal file name is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_ordinary_names_are_allowed` (parametrized) | Spaces inside a name, non-ASCII letters and names that merely start with a reserved device name stay valid. | - | unit |
| `test_spacecraft_names_whose_files_would_collide_are_refused` (parametrized) | Two spacecraft names that differ only in case or punctuation would overwrite each other's output files, so the second one is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_ground_station_names_differing_only_in_case_are_refused` | Two ground stations named alike but for case share one output file on Windows and macOS, so the scenario is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_a_ground_station_name_with_a_path_separator_is_refused` | A ground-station name is checked like a spacecraft name. | - | unit |
| `test_a_mission_sequence_longer_than_one_run_is_refused` | Audit M3: fixed-duration propagates adding up past 100 days, or a stop epoch more than 100 days after the scenario epoch, are refused at load. | - | unit |
| `test_a_report_of_a_ground_station_access_series_validates` | Audit M6: "berlin-gs.access_to_sat-1.azimuth" (offered by the Report pick-list) was refused because its first segment was taken for a spacecraft. | - | unit |
| `test_a_failed_save_leaves_the_previous_file_unchanged` | Audit M9: save truncated the file before writing, so a failure part way through destroyed the previous good file. | - | unit |
| `test_orbits_that_basilisk_cannot_fly_are_refused_at_load` (parametrized) | Audit M16: these passed validation and then misbehaved or failed in the run; they are refused when the scenario is checked. | - | unit |
| `test_comms_pointing_with_actuators_is_refused_at_load` | Audit M16: comms pointing drives an idealised torque; with actuators the run failed with "Set fsw_mode", which comms pointing forbids. | - | unit |
| `test_a_thermal_sensor_without_the_sun_is_refused_at_load` | Audit M16: a thermal sensor needs the Sun's position; the build failed. | - | unit |
| `test_a_phasing_chief_without_classical_elements_is_refused_at_load` | Audit M16: phasing reads the chief's semi-major axis from its elements. | - | unit |
| `test_monte_carlo_counts_must_be_whole_numbers` (parametrized) | Audit L29: a float, text or true/false run or thread count reached the batch unchecked. | - | unit |
| `test_dispersion_bounds_must_be_ordered_finite_numbers` (parametrized) | Audit L29: reversed or non-numeric bounds reached Basilisk's sampler. | - | unit |
| `test_dispersion_normal_needs_a_finite_mean_and_non_negative_spread` | Audit L29: a normal dispersion took any mean and a negative 1-sigma. | - | unit |
| `test_forecast_percentile_as_text_is_a_validation_error` | Audit L29: forecast_percentile given as text raised a bare ValueError instead of naming the field. | - | unit |

### `tests/test_scenario_templates.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_at_least_one_template_exists` | At least 18 templates are found, so the per-template tests below do not pass on an empty list. | - | unit |
| `test_template_loads_and_validates` (parametrized) | Every template loads and validates. | - | unit |
| `test_template_has_at_least_one_spacecraft` (parametrized) | Every template has at least one spacecraft. | - | unit |
| `test_template_has_a_substantial_description` (parametrized) | Every template's whole point is to explain a concept -- a blank or one-line description would defeat that, so this is checked directly rather than just trusting the generator script forever gets it right. | - | unit |
| `test_template_round_trips_through_save_load` (parametrized) | Every template saved and loaded again validates and is unchanged. | - | unit |
| `test_walker_constellation_template_has_six_uniquely_named_satellites` | Template 04 has six uniquely named satellites. | - | unit |
| `test_mission_sequence_template_has_a_maneuver_command` | Template 08's mission sequence has a maneuver. | - | unit |
| `test_monte_carlo_template_has_monte_carlo_enabled` | Template 09 has Monte Carlo on with at least one dispersion. | - | unit |
| `test_gravity_gradient_template_has_no_attitude_control_and_an_elongated_inertia` | Regression guard for '10's own stated lesson: gravity-gradient torque is identically zero for a spherically-symmetric inertia, and invisible behind an active controller that just rejects it as one more disturbance -- bot | - | unit |
| `test_thruster_attitude_control_template_has_only_thruster_actuators` | Template 11 has only thrusters, at least six (fewer cannot give a pure three-axis torque), and an FSW mode. | - | unit |
| `test_momentum_dumping_template_mixes_reaction_wheel_and_thruster_actuators` | Template 12 has reaction wheels and thrusters and momentum dumping with a positive limit. | - | unit |
| `test_magnetic_momentum_management_template_mixes_reaction_wheel_and_mtb_actuators` | Template 13 has reaction wheels and torque rods, and one speed bias per wheel. | - | unit |
| `test_every_template_flies_a_100_to_500_kg_spacecraft` (parametrized) | The class this app is for. | - | unit |
| `test_css_sun_heading_estimation_template_wires_use_css_estimation` | Template 14 points at the Sun from eight coarse sun sensors' estimate, with the Sun in the run and no explicit gains (the scaled default P is below 1). | - | unit |
| `test_celestial_body_pointing_template_uses_target_body_not_ground_station` | Template 15 points at the Moon, a body in the run, not at a ground station. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_transfer_template_targets_the_documented_position` | Template 16's Lambert transfer moves sat-1 to the documented position in the documented 2490 s. | - | unit |
| `test_fuel_tank_template_has_a_tank_tied_to_its_thrusters` | Template 17 has a half-full 1 kg tank and thrusters. | - | unit |
| `test_phasing_template_pairs_phasing_keeping_with_station_keeping` | Regression guard for PhasingKeepingConfig's own documented requirement (also enforced by Scenario.validate() itself) -- if a future edit to this template ever drops the paired station_keeping, this fails clearly instead  | - | unit |
| `test_leo_station_keeping_template_is_drag_driven_not_srp_driven` | The LEO counterpart to '03': altitude hold against drag, where 03 holds a GEO slot's longitude and inclination. | - | unit |
| `test_geo_template_starts_in_earths_true_equator_over_its_slot` | i = 0 in J2000 is ~0.17 deg inclined to Earth's 2030 equator: the first real run showed it as an immediate north-south burn. | - | unit |
| `test_template_descriptions_are_short_and_structured` (parametrized) | Real user feedback on the Load tab ("just awful UI/UX"): the descriptions were up to 7,000 characters of prose, including development audit history. | - | unit |
| `test_lambert_template_burns_a_quarter_orbit_in_like_basilisks_example` | examples/scenarioLambertSolver.py burns at tau/4 and arrives at tau/2. | - | unit |
| `test_sun_pointing_templates_put_their_sun_sensors_on_the_sun_face` (parametrized) | sunSafePoint turns sHatBdyCmd (default +Z) to the Sun. | - | unit |
| `test_template_descriptions_match_the_run_length` | Template 15 promised Moon pointing "for the whole orbit" over a run shorter than one orbit. | - | unit |
| `test_comms_template_has_ground_station_passes_early_in_its_run` | Real user report: template 19 showed "never ground station contact". | - | unit |
| `test_starter_templates_are_complete_starting_points` | 22 is orbit-only with a ground station and real-data drag; 23 flies the 150 kg spacecraft preset with a Mission Sequence that stops on the first Berlin pass. | - | unit |
| `test_formation_template_24_differs_from_05_only_in_its_control_law` | 24 is 05 under Basilisk's mean-element law, so the two compare directly: same spacecraft, orbits and thruster. | - | unit |
| `test_monte_carlo_template_25_disperses_the_orbit_and_the_drag_coefficient` | Template 25 disperses the orbit elements and the drag coefficient by the documented spreads, with drag on. | - | unit |

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_the_exported_flight_software_in_the_loop_reproduces_the_simulation_exactly` (parametrized) | 06 (the command is applied by a model right after the modules, in the same step), 12 (momentum dumping: the priming re-Reset) and 13 (torque rods: models between the modules): flown by the exported fsw_host, every residu | - | integration |
| `test_the_adapter_template_runs_and_is_reported_as_writing_nothing` | As generated, the adapter answers every step without commands: the run completes, the wheels get no motor torque, and every signal is listed as never written; the report names it as not exported. | - | integration |
| `test_a_program_for_another_spacecraft_or_that_exits_fails_loudly` | 07's flight software against 13's spacecraft: refused at HELLO, naming the step and ports that differ, and the program says why it was refused. | - | integration |
| `test_an_error_in_a_module_reaches_the_simulation_and_stops_the_run` | An export whose mrpFeedback has its guidance input left unconnected (one line removed from fsw_connect()): the module's own Reset check calls _bskError; fsw_host sends it as ERROR before it exits, and the run stops namin | - | integration |
| `test_fsw_host_replay_refuses_options_it_cannot_honour` | ``fsw_host replay`` with an option missing its value (``--expect`` last), a tolerance that is not a number >= 0, or an input trace with no records exits 2 naming the problem, instead of skipping the comparison and report | - | integration |
| `test_a_deadline_no_step_meets_drops_every_step` | With a 100 ns deadline every answer is late: each step counts as dropped, the commands stay as they were, and the run still completes. | - | integration |
| `test_the_c_harness_refuses_frames_that_break_the_contract` | The C side (fsw_sil.c) answers a STEP before RESET, and a frame of another contract version, with ERROR and exit status 5. | - | integration |
| `test_the_cli_runs_sil_writes_the_report_and_gates_on_the_error` | ``sil`` prints the summary and writes the JSON report and residual CSV; with --max-error it exits 2 when an output was never written. | - | integration |
| `test_the_gui_worker_reports_the_comparison_then_the_result` | RunWorker with a SIL request: sil_report (the comparison) before finished_ok (the normal results); a refused program gives sil_report with the reason, then failed. | - | integration |
| `test_a_link_that_cannot_be_opened_is_a_sil_error` | Audit L23: the listener was created outside the run's error handling, so a socket that could not be opened escaped as a raw OSError. | - | integration |

### `tests/test_sil_contract.py`

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_frames_round_trip_and_broken_headers_are_named` | A frame encodes to the 28-byte header of section 3 and back; a wrong magic, a later version, an unknown type, an oversized length and a corrupted payload are each refused with the reason. | - | unit |
| `test_hello_and_payload_blocks_round_trip_and_mismatches_are_listed` | HELLO carries the token, name, digest, step and every port; a truncated one is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_a_session_runs_lock_step_and_ends_with_bye` (parametrized) | Handshake, RESET, three STEPs each answered with that step's outputs (the fake echoes its first input), then BYE answered with BYE. | - | unit |
| `test_a_flight_software_that_does_not_match_is_refused_with_error` (parametrized) | The simulation answers ERROR (the fake receives it) and raises, naming the difference: token, step, payload layout, port count. | - | unit |
| `test_a_flight_software_speaking_another_contract_version_is_refused` | A HELLO whose header says version 2 is refused at the first frame. | - | unit |
| `test_a_late_answer_is_a_dropped_step_and_is_discarded_when_it_arrives` | With a 50 ms deadline and a 200 ms flight software, a step returns None (dropped); the late OUTPUT is recognised by its sequence number and skipped, and the next step gets its own answer. | - | unit |
| `test_a_send_never_waits_for_a_peer_that_is_itself_sending` | Both sides send more than the socket buffers hold before reading: send() keeps what arrives meanwhile for receive(), so neither waits for the other for ever. | - | unit |
| `test_a_stall_of_this_process_is_not_the_peers_silence` | The send's clock jumps 60 s just after the send starts, as when this process is stalled (a loaded machine, a debugger) while the flight software keeps reading: the socket is tried once more before the send gives up, so t | - | unit |
| `test_thousands_of_dropped_steps_never_block_the_link` (parametrized) | With a deadline no answer can meet, every step is dropped and no OUTPUT is waited for; the late OUTPUTs (more than the socket buffers hold) are still read and skipped, the flight software never blocks writing them, and t | - | unit |
| `test_a_step_without_deadline_waits_while_earlier_steps_are_still_answered` | The step timeout is "no answer at all" for that long: a step with no deadline keeps waiting while the program works through late answers to earlier steps (100 dropped steps at 30 ms each, 3 s of backlog, against a 1 s st | - | unit |
| `test_timeouts_closed_links_and_flight_software_errors_end_the_run_loudly` (parametrized) | No answer for step_s, a closed link and an ERROR from the flight software each raise SilError with the reason. | - | unit |
| `test_the_unix_socket_is_private_and_removed_afterwards` | The socket's folder is 0700 and the socket 0600 (only this user can connect); both are gone after close. | - | unit |
| `test_a_program_that_never_connects_or_exits_first_is_reported` | accept() gives up after its timeout, and at once when the program has already exited. | - | unit |
| `test_a_program_changed_after_consent_is_refused` | The runner hashes the program again just before starting it: one replaced after the user confirmed its SHA-256 is never started, and a file that is not executable is refused (security analysis S-15). | - | unit |
| `test_a_program_swapped_while_the_simulation_is_built_is_refused` | The hash is checked again right before the program starts, after the seconds the simulation takes to build: a program replaced in that window is refused and never started (security analysis S-15). | - | integration |

|---|---|---|---|

| `test_the_users_kernel_wins_over_the_install_wide_one` | installed() looks in the user's folder first, then the install's. | - | unit |
| `test_eros_is_a_central_body_with_polyhedral_gravity` | A scenario around Eros with its shape's gravity validates. | - | unit |
| `test_the_schema_refuses_what_a_run_around_eros_cannot_do` | Polyhedral gravity needs a small body; Eros cannot be a third body, have ground stations, harmonics or a run outside its kernel's span. | - | unit |
| `test_the_cli_reports_missing_eros_files` | 'small-bodies' without files installed says so and how to get them. | - | unit |

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |

| `test_noaa_fills_only_the_months_before_msfc_starts` | Between the end of CelesTrak's 45-day forecast and the first month of MSFC's table, NOAA's monthly F10.7 (with the observed-mean Ap) is all the real data there is. | - | unit |
| `test_msfc_parses_as_published_and_repeats_its_last_132_months` | AD10 Sec. | - | unit |
| `test_runs_past_2041_continue_on_the_repeated_cycle` | A run past October 2041 resolves, warning that MSFC's last 132 months repeat (ESA AD10 Sec. | - | unit |
| `test_a_run_before_the_record_is_refused_with_the_range` | A run before October 1957 is refused, giving the record's first day. | - | unit |
| `test_an_unknown_forecast_percentile_is_refused` | A forecast percentile MSFC does not publish (90) is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_a_short_table_is_not_taken_for_an_msfc_prediction` | An MSFC table cut to 40 lines is refused: a prediction needs at least 132 months. | - | unit |
| `test_a_celestrak_csv_with_forecast_rows_reads_like_the_text_file` | CelesTrak's CSV layout (OBS/PRD/PRM rows, monthly rows without Ap) gives the same days as its text file. | - | unit |
| `test_the_newer_of_the_shipped_and_downloaded_files_is_used` | A startup download observed further than the shipped file wins. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_rejects_missing_file` | Checking a file that does not exist fails, saying so. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_rejects_missing_required_columns` | A file without the required columns fails, naming one missing (AP_AVG). | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_rejects_insufficient_date_range` | A file of one day fails for a nine-day run: it does not cover the range. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_covers_range_ignores_time_of_day` | Regression test for an audit finding: covers_range used to compare a date-only (midnight) timestamp parsed from the CSV's last row against a full end_utc datetime, so a file whose last row IS the scenario's own end date  | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_detects_unsorted_dates` | A file with dates out of order fails as unsorted. | - | unit |
| `test_validate_file_detects_duplicate_dates` | A file with a date twice fails, listing the duplicate date. | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_local_file_source_reads_that_file` | The local_file source uses the given file (an excerpt of the real record), and the file it resolves to covers the run. | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_local_file_source_raises_if_missing` | The local_file source with a file that does not exist is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_local_file_source_raises_without_path` | The local_file source without a path is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_unknown_source_raises` | An unknown space-weather source is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_removed_sources_are_rejected_as_unknown` (parametrized) | "celestrak" (a runtime network fetch) and "synthetic" (a generated profile) were both removed; neither is quietly read as another source. | - | unit |
| `test_the_conservative_case_is_msfcs_predicted_cycle_at_the_95th_percentile` | Conservative is no constant: the same predicted solar cycle as nominal, at MSFC's 95th percentile, so higher every month and still rising and falling with the cycle (2030 to 2034 here). | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_has_no_activity_level_any_more` | Schema v3's historical-percentile "conservative" mode is gone (schema v4: conservative is forecast_percentile 95). | - | unit |
| `test_resolve_never_calls_fetch` | resolve() itself must NEVER touch the network, regardless of source -- fetch() is only ever reached explicitly, via gui.startup_fetch_dialog's consent-gated prompt. | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_downloads_and_caches` | fetch saves the downloaded SW-All file in the cache folder unchanged (network mocked). | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_is_a_cache_hit_without_force` | fetch with a cached file returns it without touching the network. | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_force_redownloads_even_if_cached` | fetch with force replaces a cached file with a new download. | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_rejects_unknown_dataset` | fetch of a dataset CelesTrak does not have is refused. | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_reports_network_failure` | A network error during fetch is reported as a space-weather error. | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_reports_a_disk_write_failure` | A full/read-only disk failing the write step, AFTER a successful download, must be reported as a SpaceWeatherError too -- not a raw OSError the caller (gui.startup_fetch_dialog's worker, in practice) would need its own s | - | unit |
| `test_fetch_reports_a_cache_dir_creation_failure` | A cache folder that cannot be created is reported as a space-weather error. | - | unit |
| `test_cached_fetch_path_is_none_when_nothing_fetched` | Without a fetched file, there is no cached fetch path. | - | unit |
| `test_cached_fetch_path_finds_a_real_fetch` | After a fetch, the cached fetch path is the fetched file. | - | unit |
| `test_a_cut_short_cached_file_is_written_again` | Finding F-09: a resolved file cut short (a parallel writer, or a run that died while writing) used to be reused because it existed. | - | unit |
| `test_a_failed_write_leaves_no_file_behind` | Finding F-09: the resolved file appears only complete; a write that fails part-way leaves neither it nor its temporary file. | - | unit |
| `test_a_refused_replace_keeps_the_complete_file_another_process_wrote` | F-09 on Windows: a file another process has open cannot be replaced (PermissionError). | - | unit |
| `test_a_refused_replace_of_an_incomplete_file_is_reported` | If the file held open never becomes complete, the error is raised rather than a short file being used. | - | unit |
| `test_a_download_that_is_not_celestrak_data_is_refused_and_keeps_the_old_file` | UX step 2: every download is checked before it replaces anything. | - | unit |
| `test_a_download_records_its_source_and_checksum_and_keeps_the_previous_file` | The manifest names the source, SHA-256 and dates; the replaced file is kept. | - | unit |
| `test_rollback_restores_the_previous_file` | Rollback after two imports restores the first file and its record, and a second rollback is refused: there is no earlier file. | - | unit |
| `test_import_checks_the_file_like_a_download` | Importing a file that is not a CelesTrak record is refused, as a bad download would be. | - | unit |
| `test_a_folder_or_a_binary_file_is_a_space_weather_error` | Audit M12: a local_file_path naming a folder or a binary file reached `lifetime` as a UnicodeDecodeError traceback; both are now reported. | - | unit |
| `test_a_complete_file_another_process_wrote_meanwhile_is_never_replaced` | SRelD K-14: two processes that both find the window missing both write it. | - | unit |
| `test_without_hard_links_the_file_is_moved_into_place` | A file system without hard links (OSError from os.link) still gets the file, moved into place as before. | - | unit |

| `test_malformed_tles_are_rejected_with_the_reason` (parametrized) | A changed digit, a truncated line, a swapped line or two different satellites raise TLEError naming the problem. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |

| `test_state_is_propagated_to_the_scenario_epoch` | The state is SGP4's at the SCENARIO epoch (not the TLE epoch), rotated to EME2000; the TLE age is reported. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |
| `test_timezone_aware_epoch_is_converted_to_utc` | An epoch with a UTC offset gives the same state as its UTC form. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |
| `test_sgp4_failure_is_reported` | Propagating far enough for the orbit to decay raises TLEError with SGP4's reason instead of returning a meaningless state. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |
| `test_tle_warnings_flag_old_or_broken_tles` | The plausibility checks warn about a TLE more than three days from the scenario epoch and about a malformed one; none for a fresh one. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |
| `test_service_uses_the_scenario_epoch_state` | engine.service builds a TLE spacecraft at engine.tle's state at the scenario epoch. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | integration |
| `test_precession_nutation_matches_spice_iau_1976_1980` | ERFA's J2000 -> true-of-date matrix agrees with SPICE's TRUE_EQUATOR_AND_EQUINOX_OF_DATE frame (EARTH_IAU_1976 precession, EARTH_IAU_1980 nutation) to 1e-13 rad at several epochs. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | integration |
| `test_a_tle_whose_epoch_field_is_not_a_day_of_year_is_refused` | An epoch field such as ``26280e50000000`` (a valid checksum) passed the format check, then gave a NaN epoch and failed the run with "cannot convert float NaN to integer": it is now refused naming the columns. | E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1b, E-ST-10-09C 5.3.1c, CCSDS-502.0-B-3 4.2.4.9 | unit |

| `test_two_body_propagation_conserves_energy_and_angular_momentum` | A two-body run conserves specific energy and angular momentum within their tolerances. | E-ST-10-04C 4.2.1a | integration |
| `test_two_body_orbit_elements_match_input_classical_elements` | Regression test for ResultSet's ``{spacecraft}.orbit_elements.*`` series (osculating Keplerian elements, added alongside position/ velocity). | E-ST-10-04C 4.2.1a | integration |

| `test_dangling_reference_nested_in_if_is_collected` | A missing spacecraft named inside an if is listed. | - | unit |
| `test_bad_first_command_is_not_reported_twice` | Regression test for a real audit finding: Scenario.validate() ALSO validates mission_sequence commands (raise-fast, stopping at the first bad one) in addition to the resource-level checks that run before it -- so when ev | - | unit |
| `test_dangling_reference_in_first_command_is_not_reported_twice` | A missing spacecraft named in the first command is listed once. | - | unit |
| `test_valid_mission_sequence_referencing_real_spacecraft_has_no_errors` | A mission sequence naming only real spacecraft has no problems. | - | unit |

| Test case | Purpose | Requirements | Level |
|---|---|---|---|
| `test_station_keeping_with_vizard_save_file_does_not_crash` | The real bug: a station-keeping spacecraft (the only controller that creates a GenericStorage panel -- see engine.vizard's "Live-data panels" docstring section) run with Vizard output enabled used to crash within the fir | - | integration |
| `test_generic_storage_and_sensor_lists_are_retained_after_build` | The actual fix: enable_vizard()'s GenericStorage/GenericSensor panel objects (and their embedded ReadFunctor readers) must survive past enable_vizard() returning, same as access_indicator_bridges -- engine.service.Simula | - | integration |
| `test_enable_vizard_with_a_ground_station_does_not_crash` | Real user report, reproduced exactly against a real Basilisk build (not just reasoned through) before being fixed here: running ANY scenario with a ground station AND Vizard output enabled crashed every time with ``Syste | - | integration |
| `test_enable_vizard_with_comms_pointing_builds_the_three_new_panels` | Real user feedback: the GUI's mission dashboard (``gui.mission_ dashboard_widget``, template 19's live telemetry) "shall also be in the vizard live visualization, not only in the GUI itself". | - | integration |
| `test_trail_and_ground_tracks_are_off_unless_asked_for` (parametrized) | Real user feedback on a 26-day formation run in Vizard: "visually this is very confusing and not really clear" -- a solid cyan band built from hundreds of overlapping flown-path/ground-track lines. | - | integration |
| `test_ground_station_cone_reaches_the_highest_orbit` | Without a range, Vizard drew each station's cone 0.4 planet radii tall, so a ~160 deg cone's rim reached ~2.3 radii out: a disc wider than Earth around every station (Vizard's FullLocationMethods.cs). | - | integration |
